satisfies the following bound: for any
K\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
there is
s_{0}>0
such that for any
s\geq s_{0}
,
0<r<r_{0}(s,K)
small enough and any
Z\in B_{s_{0}}^{K}(I;r)\cap C^{K}_{*\mathbb{R}}(I;\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2}))
one has

2^{-1}\|Z\|_{k,s}\leq\|Z+R_{\leq N}(Z)Z\|_{k,s}\leq 2\|Z\|_{k,s}\,,\quad\forall 0\leq k\leq K\,.

(2.40)

Fourier representation of
m
-operators.
We may also represent a matrix of operators

M(U)=\begin{pmatrix}M^{+}_{+}(U)&M^{-}_{+}(U)\\
M^{+}_{-}(U)&M^{-}_{-}(U)\end{pmatrix}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

(2.41)

through their Fourier matrix elements, see (
2.6
), writing

\displaystyle M(U)V=\begin{pmatrix}(M(U)V)^{+}\\
(M(U)V)^{-}\end{pmatrix},\quad(M(U)V)^{\sigma}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k)\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{p+2}\\
(\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime})\in\{\pm\}^{p+1}\\
\sigma k=\vec{\sigma}_{p}\cdot\vec{\jmath}_{p}+\sigma^{\prime}j\end{subarray}}M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}u_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p}}v^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{j}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma kx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,,

(2.42)

where
1
1

1

Given
u=\begin{pmatrix}u_{1}\\
u_{2}\end{pmatrix},\,v=\begin{pmatrix}v_{1}\\
v_{2}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
we set
u\cdot v:=u_{1}v_{1}+u_{2}v_{2}
.

\displaystyle M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}:=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}M\Big(\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{1}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{1}j_{1}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},\dots,\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{p}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{p}j_{p}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big)\Big[\frac{\mathtt{q}^{\sigma^{\prime}}e^{{\rm i}\sigma^{\prime}jx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big]\cdot\mathtt{q}^{\sigma}\frac{e^{-{\rm i}\sigma kx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,{\rm d}x

(2.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}M^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\sigma}\Big(\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{1}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{1}j_{1}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},\dots,\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{p}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{p}j_{p}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big)\Big[\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma^{\prime}jx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big]\,\frac{e^{-{\rm i}\sigma kx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,{\rm d}x\,\in\mathbb{C}\ ,

and
{\mathtt{q}}^{\pm}
are defined in (
2.7
).
In (
2.42
) we have exploited the translation invariance property
(
2.33
) which implies that if
M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\not=0
then

\sigma k=\vec{\sigma}_{p}\cdot\vec{\jmath}_{p}+\sigma^{\prime}j\,.

(2.44)

Note also that since
M
is symmetric in the internal entries, the coefficients
M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
in
(
2.43
) satisfy the following symmetric property:
for any permutation
\pi
of
\{1,\ldots,p\}
, it results

M_{j_{\pi(1)},\ldots,j_{\pi(p)},j,k}^{\sigma_{\pi(1)},\ldots,\sigma_{\pi(p)},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}=M_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{p},j,k}^{\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\,.

(2.45)

The operator
M(U)
is real-to-real, according to definition (
2.12
), if and only if its coefficients fulfill

\overline{M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}}=M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{-\vec{\sigma}_{p},-\sigma^{\prime},-\sigma}\ .

(2.46)

The matrix entries of the transpose operator
M(U)^{\top}

with respect to the non-degenerate bilinear form (
2.4
) are

\displaystyle(M^{\top})_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}M^{\top}\Big(\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{1}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{1}j_{1}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},\dots,\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{p}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{p}j_{p}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big)\Big[\frac{\mathtt{q}^{\sigma^{\prime}}e^{{\rm i}\sigma^{\prime}jx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big]\cdot\frac{\mathtt{q}^{\sigma}e^{-{\rm i}\sigma kx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,{\rm d}x

(2.47)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}M\Big(\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{1}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{1}j_{1}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},\dots,\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{p}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{p}j_{p}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big)\Big[\frac{\mathtt{q}^{\sigma}e^{-{\rm i}\sigma kx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big]\cdot\frac{\mathtt{q}^{\sigma^{\prime}}e^{{\rm i}\sigma^{\prime}jx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,{\rm d}x=M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},-k,-j}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}\,.

One can directly verify that
(M^{\top})_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
fulfill (
2.44
), (
2.45
). If
M(U)
is real-to-real (i.e. (
2.46
) holds) then
M(U)^{\top}

is real-to-real as well.

Lemma 2.9
.

(Characterization of
m
-operators in Fourier basis)

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
. A real-to-real linear operator
M(U)
as in (
2.42
)-(
2.43
)
is a matrix of
p
-homogeneous
m
-operators in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
if and only if its coefficients
M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}

satisfy (
2.44
), (
2.45
), (
2.46
) and
there exist
\mu>0
and
C>0
such that for any
(\vec{\jmath}_{p},j)\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{p+1}
,
(\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma)\in\{\pm\}^{p+1}
,

|M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}|\leq C{\rm max}_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\mu}\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{m}\,.

(2.48)

Proof.

Let
M(U)
be a matrix of
m
-operators in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
Then by (
2.43
), applying Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and recalling (
2.8
) we get

\displaystyle|M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|{M^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\sigma}}\Big(\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{1}}\Pi_{|j_{1}|}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{1}j_{1}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},\dots,\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{p}}\Pi_{|j_{p}|}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{p}j_{p}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big)\Big[\Pi_{|j|}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma^{\prime}jx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{omoresti2}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{p},\epsilon\in\{\pm\}\\
n_{0}=\epsilon_{1}|j_{1}|+\dots+\epsilon_{p}|j_{p}|+\epsilon|j|\end{subarray}}\Big\|\Pi_{n_{0}}{M^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\sigma}}\Big(\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{1}}\Pi_{|j_{1}|}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{1}j_{1}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},\dots,\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{p}}\Pi_{|j_{p}|}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{p}j_{p}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big)\Big[\Pi_{|j|}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma^{\prime}jx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big]\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{omomap}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle C2^{p+1}{\rm max}_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\mu}\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{m}

proving (
2.48
).
Viceversa suppose that
M(U)
is an operator as in
(
2.41
)-(
2.43
) with coefficients satisfying (
2.48
). Then, for any

\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\{\pm\}
,

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n_{0}}{M^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{\sigma}}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{p}}U_{p})\Pi_{n_{p+1}}v^{\sigma^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}}=\Big\|\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=\pm n_{1},\dots,j_{p}=\pm n_{p}\\
j=\pm n_{p+1},\,k=\pm n_{0}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}(u_{1})_{j_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}}\cdots(u_{p})_{j_{p}}^{\sigma_{p}}v^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{j}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma kx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{smoocara}}}{{\leq}}C\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=\pm n_{1},\dots,j_{p}=\pm n_{p}\\
j=\pm n_{p+1},\,k=\pm n_{0}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!{\rm max}_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\mu}\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{m}|(u_{1})_{j_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}}|\cdots|(u_{p})_{j_{p}}^{\sigma_{p}}||v^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq C2^{p+2}{\rm max}_{2}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{p},n_{p+1}\}^{\mu}\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{p},n_{p+1}\}^{m}\prod_{\ell=1}^{p}\|\Pi_{n_{\ell}}U_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}\|\Pi_{n_{p+1}}v\|_{L^{2}}

proving (
2.31
).
∎

The transpose of a matrix of
m
-operators is a
m^{\prime}
-operator.

Lemma 2.10
.

(Transpose of
m
-Operators)

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
M(U)
is a matrix of
p
–homogeneous

m
-operators in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
then

M(U)^{\top}
(where the transpose is computed with respect to the non-degenerate bilinear form (
2.4
))
is a matrix of
p
-homogeneous operators in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{p}^{m^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for some
m^{\prime}\geq\max(m,0)
.

If in addition
there exists
C>1
such that

M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},k,j}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}\neq 0\quad\Rightarrow\quad C^{-1}|k|\leq|j|\leq C|k|

(2.49)

then
M(U)^{\top}\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{p}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

By (
2.47
),
(
2.48
) (applied to
M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},-k,-j}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}
) and since, by (
2.44
),

|k|\leq(p+1)\max\{|j|,|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|\}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle|(M^{\top})_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}|

\displaystyle\lesssim{\rm max}_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|k|\}^{\mu}\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|k|\}^{m}

(2.50)

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\max(m,0)+\mu}

proving (
2.48
) for
M(U)^{\top}
with
m\leadsto m^{\prime}:=\max(m,0)+\mu
.

If in addition (
2.49
) holds true then

\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|k|\}^{m}\sim\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{m}
for any
m\in\mathbb{R}

and similarly

\max_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|k|\}^{\mu}\sim\max_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\mu}
for any

\mu\geq 0
.
We deduce by (
2.50
) that
M(U)^{\top}
is in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{m}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
∎

Remark 2.11
.

(Transpose of Smoothing operators)

If
R(U)
is a matrix of smoothing operators in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

and
the spectral condition (
2.49
) holds true, then

R(U)^{\top}
is a smoothing operator in the same class. Without the spectral condition
(
2.49
) this might fail: for example consider
R(U)
such that its transpose is

R(U_{1},\ldots,U_{p})^{\top}V:={{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(A(V,U_{1},\ldots,U_{p-1})\right)}U_{p}\ ,\ \ A(\cdot)\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\mu}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\ .

(2.51)

As a consequence of Lemma
2.21
below, we have that

R(U)=[R(U)^{\top}]^{\top}
is in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\varrho\geq 0
, but
R(U)^{\top}
in (
2.51
) is a
\mu
-operator.

We conclude this subsection with
the paralinearization of the product (see e.g. Lemma 7.2 in
[
16
]
).

Lemma 2.12
.

(Bony paraproduct decomposition)

Let
u_{1},u_{2}
be functions in
H^{\sigma}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C})
with

\sigma>\frac{1}{2}
. Then

u_{1}u_{2}={{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(u_{1}\right)}u_{2}+{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(u_{2}\right)}u_{1}+R_{1}(u_{1})u_{2}+R_{2}(u_{2})u_{1}

(2.52)

where for
j=1,2
,
R_{j}
is a homogeneous smoothing operator in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{1}
for any
\varrho\geq 0
.

Composition theorems.

Let

\sigma(D_{x},D_{\xi},D_{y},D_{\eta}):=D_{\xi}D_{y}-D_{x}D_{\eta}

where
D_{x}:=\frac{1}{{\rm i}}\partial_{x}
and
D_{\xi},D_{y},D_{\eta}
are similarly defined.
The following is Definition 3.11 in
[
16
]
.

Definition 2.13
.

(Asymptotic expansion of composition symbol)

Let
p
,
p^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
K,K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
K^{\prime}\leq K
,
\varrho\geq 0
,
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
,
r>0
.
Consider symbols
a\in\Sigma\Gamma_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}[r,N]
and
b\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime}}[r,N]
. For
U
in
B_{\sigma}^{K}(I;r)

we define, for
\varrho<\sigma-s_{0}
, the symbol

(a\#_{\varrho}b)(U;t,x,\xi):=\sum_{k=0}^{\varrho}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\sigma(D_{x},D_{\xi},D_{y},D_{\eta})\right)^{k}\Big[a(U;t,x,\xi)b(U;t,y,\eta)\Big]_{|_{\begin{subarray}{c}x=y,\xi=\eta\end{subarray}}}

(2.53)

modulo symbols in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m+m^{\prime}-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[r,N]
.

\bullet

The symbol
a\#_{\varrho}b
belongs to
\Sigma\Gamma^{m+m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[r,N]
.

\bullet

We have that

a\#_{\varrho}b=ab+\frac{1}{2{\rm i}}\{a,b\}
up to a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m+m^{\prime}-2}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[r,N]
,
where

\{a,b\}:=\partial_{\xi}a\partial_{x}b-\partial_{x}a\partial_{\xi}b

denotes the Poisson bracket.

\bullet

If
c
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m^{\prime\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime}}[r,N]
then

a\#_{\varrho}b\#_{\varrho}c+c\#_{\varrho}b\#_{\varrho}a-2abc
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m+m^{\prime}+m^{\prime\prime}-2}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}+p^{\prime\prime}}[r,N]
.

\bullet

\overline{a^{\vee}}\#_{\varrho}\overline{b^{\vee}}=\overline{a\#_{\varrho}b}^{\vee}
where
a^{\vee}
is defined in (
2.27
).

The following result is proved in Proposition
3.12
in
[
16
]
.

Proposition 2.14
.

(Composition of Bony-Weyl operators)

Let
p,q,N,K,K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
K^{\prime}\leq K
,
\varrho\geq 0
,
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
,
r>0
.
Consider symbols

a\in\Sigma{\Gamma}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
and
b\in\Sigma{\Gamma}^{m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},q}[r,N]
.
Then

{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(a(U;t,x,\xi)\right)}\circ{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(b(U;t,x,\xi)\right)}-{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left((a\#_{\varrho}b)(U;t,x,\xi)\right)}

(2.54)

is a smoothing operator in
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho+m+m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p+q}[r,N]
.

We now prove other composition results concerning
m
-operators.

Proposition 2.15
.

(Compositions of
m
-operators)

Let
p,p^{\prime},N,K,K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
K^{\prime}\leq K
and
r>0
. Let
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then

(i)

If
M(U;t)
is in

\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
and
M^{\prime}(U;t)
is in

\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime}}[r,N]
then the composition

M(U;t)\circ M^{\prime}(U;t)

is in
\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m+\max(m^{\prime},0)}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[r,N]
.

(ii)

If
M(U)
is a homogeneous
m
-operator in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}

and
M^{(\ell)}(U;t)
,
\ell=1,\dots,p+1
, are matrices of
m_{\ell}
-operators in

\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m_{\ell}}_{K,K^{\prime},q_{\ell}}[r,N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
with
m_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}
,

q_{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
then

M(M^{(1)}(U;t)U,\ldots,M^{(p)}(U;t)U)M^{(p+1)}(U;t)

belongs to
\Sigma\mathcal{M}_{K,K^{\prime},p+\overline{q}}^{m+\overline{m}}[r,N]
with
\overline{m}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{p+1}\max(m_{\ell},0)
and
\overline{q}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{p+1}q_{\ell}
.

(iii)

If
M(U;t)
is in
{\mathcal{M}}_{K,0,p}^{m}[\breve{r}]
for any
\breve{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)
belongs to
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},0}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
,
then
M({\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U;t)
is in
\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r]
.

(iv)

Let
c
be a homogeneous symbol in
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{p}^{m}
and
M^{(\ell)}(U;t)
,
\ell=1,\dots,p
, be operators in

\Sigma\mathcal{M}_{K,K^{\prime},q_{\ell}}[r,N]
with
q_{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
Then

U\rightarrow b(U;t,x,\xi):=c(M^{(1)}(U;t)U,\ldots,M^{(p)}(U;t)U;t,x,\xi)

is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p+\overline{q}}[r,N]
with
\overline{q}:=q_{1}+\dots+q_{p}
and

{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}(c(W_{1},\ldots,W_{p};t,x,\xi))_{|W_{\ell}=M^{(\ell)}(U;t)U}={{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}(b(U;t,x,\xi))+R(U;t)

where
R(U;t)
is a smoothing operator in

\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p+\overline{q}}[r,N]

for any
\varrho\geq 0
.

Proof.

Proof of
(ii)
:

It is sufficient to prove the thesis for
m_{\ell}\geq 0
otherwise we regard
M^{(\ell)}(U;t)
as a
0
-operator in
\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
.
We decompose, for any
\ell=1,\dots,p+1
,
M^{(\ell)}=\sum_{a=q_{\ell}}^{N}M_{a}^{(\ell)}+M^{(\ell)}_{>N}
as in (
2.35
).
Given integers
a_{\ell}\in[q_{\ell},N]
,
\ell=1,\dots p+1
, we use the notation
\overline{a}_{\ell}:=a_{1}+\dots+a_{\ell}+\ell
and
\overline{a}_{0}:=0
.
Note that

\overline{a}_{p+1}-1=a_{1}+\ldots+a_{p+1}+p\geq\overline{q}+p
.
We also denote the vector with
a_{\ell}
elements

\mathcal{U}_{\ell}:=(U_{\overline{a}_{\ell-1}+1},\ldots,U_{\overline{a}_{\ell}-1})\ ,\quad\ell=1,\ldots,p+1\,.

By multi-linearity we have to show on the one hand that,
if
\overline{a}_{p+1}-1\leq N
then

M\Big(M^{(1)}_{a_{1}}(\mathcal{U}_{1})U_{a_{1}+1},\dots,M^{(p)}_{a_{p}}(\mathcal{U}_{p})U_{\overline{a}_{p}}\Big)M^{(p+1)}_{a_{p+1}}(\mathcal{U}_{p+1})

(2.55)

is a homogeneous operator in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}^{m+\overline{m}}_{\overline{a}_{p+1}-1}
. On the other hand, if
\overline{a}_{p+1}-1\geq N+1
then

M\Big(M^{(1)}_{a_{1}}(U,\dots,U)U,\dots,M^{(p)}_{a_{p}}(U,\dots,U)U\Big)M^{(p+1)}_{a_{p+1}}(U,\dots,U)\quad

(2.56)

is a non–homogeneous operator in
\mathcal{M}^{m+\overline{m}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
, having included for notational convenience the inhomogeneous term as
M^{(\ell)}_{N+1}(U,\dots,U):=M^{(\ell)}_{>N}(U;t)
which belongs to

\mathcal{M}^{m_{\ell}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.

We first study (
2.55
).
First of all, using the notation

\vec{n}_{\ell}:=(n_{\overline{a}_{\ell-1}+1},\ldots,n_{\overline{a}_{\ell}-1})

one has

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n_{0}}M\Big(M^{(1)}_{a_{1}}(\Pi_{\vec{n}_{1}}\mathcal{U}_{1})\Pi_{n_{\overline{a}_{1}}}U_{\overline{a}_{1}},\dots,M^{(p)}_{a_{p}}(\Pi_{\vec{n}_{p}}\mathcal{U}_{p})\Pi_{n_{\overline{a}_{p}}}U_{\overline{a}_{p}}\Big)M^{(p+1)}_{a_{p+1}}(\Pi_{\vec{n}_{p+1}}\mathcal{U}_{p+1})\Pi_{n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}}U_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{n^{\prime}_{1},\dots,n_{p+1}^{\prime}}\!\!\!\!\!\|\Pi_{n_{0}}M\Big(\Pi_{n^{\prime}_{1}}M^{(1)}_{a_{1}}(\Pi_{\vec{n}_{1}}\mathcal{U}_{1})\Pi_{n_{\overline{a}_{1}}}U_{\overline{a}_{1}},\dots,\Pi_{n^{\prime}_{p}}M^{(p)}_{a_{p}}(\Pi_{\vec{n}_{p}}\mathcal{U}_{p})\Pi_{n_{\overline{a}_{p}}}U_{\overline{a}_{p}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\circ\Pi_{n^{\prime}_{p+1}}M^{(p+1)}_{a_{p+1}}(\Pi_{\vec{n}_{p+1}}\mathcal{U}_{p+1})\Pi_{n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}}U_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\|_{L^{2}}\,.

(2.57)

Thanks to the conditions (
2.32
) for
M
and
M^{(\ell)}

the indices in the above sum satisfy, for some choice of signs
\sigma_{b},\epsilon_{j}
,
b=1,\dots,\overline{a}_{p+1}
,
j=1,\dots,p+1
,
the restrictions

n_{0}=\sum_{j=1}^{p+1}\epsilon_{j}n^{\prime}_{j}\,,\quad n^{\prime}_{\ell}=\sum_{b=\overline{a}_{\ell-1}+1}^{\overline{a}_{\ell}}\sigma_{b}n_{b}\,,\quad\ell=1,\dots,p+1\,.

(2.58)

As a consequence (
2.55
) satisfies the corresponding condition (
2.32
) and

n^{\prime}_{\ell}\lesssim\max\{\vec{n}_{\ell},n_{\overline{a}_{\ell}}\}\ ,\qquad\forall\ell=1,\dots,p+1\ ,

(2.59)

then we have

\displaystyle{\rm max}\{n^{\prime}_{1},\dots,n^{\prime}_{p+1}\}\lesssim{\rm max}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}

(2.60)

\displaystyle{\rm max}_{2}\{n^{\prime}_{1},\dots,n^{\prime}_{p+1}\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{denaco34}}}{{\lesssim}}{\rm max}_{2}\left\{\max\{\vec{n}_{1},n_{\overline{a}_{1}}\},\ldots,\max\{\vec{n}_{p+1},n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}\right\}\lesssim{\rm max}_{2}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}

where in the last inequality we used that

\{n_{1},\ldots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}
is the disjoint union of the sets

\{\vec{n}_{\ell},n_{\overline{a}_{\ell}}\}_{\ell=1,\ldots,p+1}
.

Using (
2.58
), (
2.31
) for
M
and
M^{(\ell)}
we get, with
\vec{n}^{\prime}:=(n_{1}^{\prime},\dots,n^{\prime}_{p+1})

\displaystyle\eqref{inininni35}\lesssim{\rm max}_{2}\{\vec{n}^{\prime}\}^{\mu}{\rm max}\{\vec{n}^{\prime}\}^{m}\prod_{\ell=1}^{p+1}{\rm max}_{2}\{\vec{n}_{\ell},n_{\overline{a}_{\ell}}\}^{\mu_{\ell}}{\rm max}\{\vec{n}_{\ell},n_{\overline{a}_{\ell}}\}^{m_{\ell}}\prod_{b=1}^{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\|\Pi_{n_{b}}U_{b}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{denaco35},\eqref{mono:max2},m_{\ell}\geq 0}}{{\lesssim}}{\rm max}_{2}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}^{\overline{\mu}}\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}^{\overline{m}}{\rm max}\{\vec{n}^{\prime}\}^{m}\prod_{b=1}^{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\|\Pi_{n_{b}}U_{b}\|_{L^{2}},

(2.61)

where
\overline{\mu}:=\mu+\mu_{1}+\dots+\mu_{p+1}
and recall
\overline{m}=m_{1}+\dots+m_{p+1}
.
We claim that

{\rm max}\{\vec{n}^{\prime}\}^{m}\lesssim{\rm max}_{2}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}^{\mu^{\prime\prime}}\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}^{m}

(2.62)

for some
\mu^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
.
Then (
2.31
), (
2.61
) and (
2.62
) imply that the operator in (
2.55
) belongs to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{\overline{a}_{p+1}-1}^{m+\overline{m}}
.

We now prove (
2.62
).
If
m\geq 0
it follows by
(
2.60
) with
\mu^{\prime\prime}=0
.
So from now on we consider
m<0
.
We fix
\overline{\ell}
such that

\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}={\rm max}\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}

(2.63)

and we distinguish two cases:

Case
1
:

n^{\prime}_{\overline{\ell}}\geq\frac{1}{2}{\rm max}\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}
. In this case

{\rm max}\{\vec{n}^{\prime}\}^{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m<0}}{{\leq}}(n^{\prime}_{\overline{\ell}})^{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Case}\,1}}{{\leq}}2^{|m|}\,{\rm max}\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}^{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{scelgouguali}}}{{=}}2^{|m|}\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}^{m}

which proves (
2.62
) with
\mu^{\prime\prime}=0
.

Case
2
:

n^{\prime}_{\overline{\ell}}<\frac{1}{2}{\rm max}\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}
.
In view of the momentum condition (
2.58
),

\displaystyle\max\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}

\displaystyle\leq a_{\overline{\ell}}{\rm max}_{2}\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}+n^{\prime}_{\overline{\ell}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Case}\,2}}{{\leq}}a_{\overline{\ell}}{\rm max}_{2}\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}+\frac{1}{2}{\rm max}\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}

and consequently

\max\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}\leq 2a_{\overline{\ell}}{\rm max}_{2}\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}\,.

(2.64)

Then, since
m<0
,

\displaystyle\max\{\vec{n}^{\prime}\}^{m}

\displaystyle\leq 1\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{smoopercaso35}}}{{\leq}}(2a_{\overline{\ell}})^{|m|}\ {\rm max}_{2}\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}^{|m|}\ \max\{\vec{n}_{\overline{\ell}},\ n_{\overline{a}_{\overline{\ell}}}\}^{m}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{scelgouguali},\eqref{mono:max2}}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle(2a_{\overline{\ell}})^{|m|}{\rm max}_{2}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}^{|m|}\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{\overline{a}_{p+1}}\}^{m}

which proves (
2.62
) with
\mu^{\prime\prime}=|m|
.
This concludes the proof of (
2.62
)
and then that the operator in (
2.55
) is in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{\overline{a}_{p+1}-1}^{m+\overline{m}}
.

Now we prove that the operator in (
2.56
)
is in
\mathcal{M}^{m+\overline{m}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.
We have to verify (
2.34
) with
m\leadsto m+\overline{m}
and
p\leadsto N+1
.
For simplicity we denote
M^{(\ell)}_{a_{\ell}}(U)=M^{(\ell)}_{a_{\ell}}(U,\ldots,U)
.
First we apply (
2.39
)
to
M(U)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{m}_{p}

(with

U_{\ell}\leadsto M^{(\ell)}_{a_{\ell}}(U)U
,

v\leadsto M^{(p+1)}_{a_{p+1}}(U)v
and

s\leadsto s-\overline{m}
) getting, for any
k=0,\ldots,K-K^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}\big(\eqref{nonomo35}v\big)\|_{\dot{H}^{s-m-\overline{m}-\frac{3}{2}k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{K}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{k_{1}+\dots+k_{p+1}=k}\!\!\!\!\!\Big({\|M^{(p+1)}_{a_{p+1}}(U)v\|}_{k_{p+1},s-\overline{m}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{p}\ {\|M^{(\ell)}_{a_{\ell}}(U)U\|}_{k_{\ell},s_{0}}

(2.65)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!+{\|M^{(p+1)}_{a_{p+1}}(U)v\|}_{k_{p+1},s_{0}}\sum_{\overline{\ell}=1}^{p}{\|M^{(\overline{\ell})}_{a_{\overline{\ell}}}(U)U\|}_{k_{\overline{\ell}},s-\overline{m}}\prod_{\ell=1\atop\ell\neq\overline{\ell}}^{p}{\|M^{(\ell)}_{a_{\ell}}(U)U\|}_{k_{\ell},s_{0}}\Big)\,.

(2.66)

Then we estimate line (
2.65
) where,
by Lemma
2.8
,
each
M^{(\ell)}_{a_{\ell}}(U)
is in
\mathcal{M}^{m_{\ell}}_{K,0,a_{\ell}}[r]

and

M^{(\ell)}_{N+1}(U)
belongs to

\mathcal{M}^{m_{\ell}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
. For any
\ell=1,\ldots,p
, using
(
2.34
) (with
m\leadsto m_{\ell}
and
p\leadsto a_{\ell}
)
we bound (since
k_{\ell}\leq k-k_{p+1}
and
0\leq k\leq K-K^{\prime}
)

\displaystyle{\|M^{(\ell)}_{a_{\ell}}(U)U\|}_{k_{\ell},s_{0}}\leq C{\|U\|}_{k_{\ell}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{a_{\ell}+1}\leq C{\|U\|}_{k-k_{p+1}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{a_{\ell}+1}\,,

(2.67)

\displaystyle{\|M^{(p+1)}_{a_{p+1}}(U)v\|}_{k_{p+1},s-\overline{m}}\leq C\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}\leq k_{p+1}}{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s}{\|U\|}_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{a_{p+1}}+{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s_{0}}{\|U\|}_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{a_{p+1}-1}{\|U\|}_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq C\sum_{k^{\prime\prime}\leq k_{p+1}}{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{a_{p+1}}+{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s_{0}}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{a_{p+1}-1}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s}

since
k^{\prime}\leq k-k^{\prime\prime}
(being
k_{p+1}\leq k
).
By (
2.67
) and since

k-k_{p+1}+K^{\prime}\leq k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime}
, we get

(
2.65
)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{K,s}\sum_{k^{\prime\prime}\leq k}{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{a_{1}+\dots+a_{p+1}+p}+{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s_{0}}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{a_{1}+\dots+a_{p+1}+p-1}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{K,s}\sum_{k^{\prime\prime}\leq k}{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{N+1}+{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s_{0}}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{N}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s}

(2.68)

because
a_{1}+\dots+a_{p+1}+p=\overline{a}_{p+1}-1\geq N+1

(for
\|U\|_{K,s_{0}+\overline{m}}<1
).
Regarding (
2.66
), we first bound by
(
2.34
) (and (
2.67
) with
s=s_{0}+\overline{m}
)

\displaystyle{\|M^{(\overline{\ell})}_{a_{\overline{\ell}}}(U)U\|}_{k_{\overline{\ell}},s-\overline{m}}\leq C{\|U\|}_{k_{\overline{\ell}}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{a_{\overline{\ell}}}{\|U\|}_{k_{\overline{\ell}}+K^{\prime},s}\leq C{\|U\|}_{k-k_{p+1}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{a_{\overline{\ell}}}{\|U\|}_{k-k_{p+1}+K^{\prime},s}

\displaystyle{\|M^{(p+1)}_{a_{p+1}}(U)v\|}_{k_{p+1},s_{0}}\leq C\sum_{k^{\prime\prime}\leq k_{p+1}}{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}\ {\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{a_{p+1}}

and, proceeding similarly to the previous computation, we deduce

\eqref{due2}\lesssim_{K,s}\sum_{k^{\prime\prime}\leq k}{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{N}{\|U\|}_{k-k^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s}\,.

(2.69)

Hence (
2.68
), (
2.69
) imply that for any
k=0,\ldots,K-K^{\prime}

\|\partial_{t}^{k}\big(\eqref{nonomo35}v\big)\|_{\dot{H}^{s-m-\overline{m}-\frac{3}{2}k}}\lesssim_{K,s}\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\!\!\!\!{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s}{\|U\|}_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{N+1}+{\|v\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}{\|U\|}_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}+\overline{m}}^{N}{\|U\|}_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s}

(2.70)

proving that the operator (
2.56
)
satisfies (
2.34
) with
m\leadsto m+\overline{m}
,
p\leadsto N+1

and
s_{0}\leadsto s_{0}+\overline{m}
.

Proof of
(i)
:

Decomposing
M=\sum_{q=p}^{N}M_{q}+M_{>N}
and
M^{\prime}=\sum_{q=p^{\prime}}^{N}M^{\prime}_{q}+M^{\prime}_{>N}
as in (
2.35
), by item
(ii)
we deduce that

M_{q_{1}}(U)M^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(U)

is in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}^{m+\max{\{m^{\prime},0\}}}_{q_{1}+q_{2}}
if
q_{1}+q_{2}\leq N
,
and
M_{q_{1}}(U)M^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(U)
,
q_{1}+q_{2}\geq N+1

and
M_{q_{1}}(U)M^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t)
,
q_{1}=p,\dots,N,
are
in
\mathcal{M}^{m+\max\{m^{\prime},0\}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.
Furthermore, proceeding as in the proof of (
2.70
), one shows that

M_{>N}(U;t)M^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(U)
,
q_{2}=p^{\prime},\dots,N
, and

M_{>N}(U;t)M^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t)

are operators in
\mathcal{M}^{m+\max\{m^{\prime},0\}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.

Proof of
(iii)
:

As
{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},0}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, for any

U\in B^{K^{\prime}}_{s_{0}}(I;r)
,
the function
{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U\in B^{0}_{s_{0}}(I;Cr)
,
for some
C\geq 1
. Hence the composition
M({\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U)
is a well defined operator. Moreover, for any
U\in B_{s_{0}}^{K}(I;r(\sigma))
, we have the quantitative estimate

{\|{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U\|}_{k,\sigma}\lesssim{\|U\|}_{k,\sigma}
, for

k=0,\ldots,K-K^{\prime}
and
\sigma\geq s_{0}
.
To bound the time derivatives of
M({\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U)
we use (
2.34
) and the previous estimate to get,
for any
k=0,\ldots,K-K^{\prime}

\displaystyle{\|\partial_{t}^{k}\left(M({\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U)V\right)\|}_{s-\frac{3}{2}k-m}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}{\|{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U\|}_{k^{\prime},s_{0}}^{p}{\|V\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s}+{\|{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U\|}_{k^{\prime},s}{\|{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U\|}_{k^{\prime},s_{0}}^{p-1}{\|V\|}_{k^{\prime\prime},s_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\!\!\!\!\|V\|_{k^{\prime\prime},s}\|{U}\|_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{p}+\|{V}\|_{k^{\prime\prime},s_{0}}\|U\|_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{p-1}\|{U}\|_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s}

proving (
2.34
).

Proof of
(iv)
:
It follows, in view of Remark
2.6
,
by Proposition 3.17-
(i)
and Proposition 3.18 in
[
16
]
.
∎

We shall use the following facts which follow by
Proposition
2.15
.

\bullet

If
a(U;t,x,\xi)
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}[r,N]
and
U
is a solution of
\partial_{t}U=M(U;t)U
for some
M(U;t)
in
\Sigma\mathcal{M}_{K,0,0}[r,N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, then
\partial_{t}a(U;t,x,\xi)
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma_{K,K^{\prime}+1,p}^{m}[r,N]
.

\bullet
If
R(U;t)
is a smoothing operator
in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
and
U
is a solution of
\partial_{t}U=M(U;t)U
for some
M(U;t)
in
\Sigma\mathcal{M}_{K,0,0}[r,N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, then
\partial_{t}R(U;t)
is a smoothing operator
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime}+1,p}[r,N]
.

\bullet

If
M(U;t)
is in

\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
and
R(U;t)
is in

\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime}}[r,N]
then the composition

M(U;t)\circ R(U;t)

is in
\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[r,N]
, and so it is not a smoothing map.

2.2
Spectrally localized maps

We introduce the notion of a “spectrally localized” map.
The class
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}
denotes
p
-linear

m
-operators
with the spectral support similar to a para–differential operator (compare (
2.25
) and (
2.71
)), i.e. the “ internal frequencies” are controlled by the “external” ones which are equivalent.
On the other hand the class
\mathcal{S}_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}
contains non-homogeneous

m
-operators
which vanish at degree at least
p
in
U
,
are
(K-K^{\prime})
-times differentiable in
t
, and satisfy estimates similar to
para–differential operators, see (
2.72
).
These maps include para–differential operators, smoothing remainders which come from compositions of para–differential operators (see (
2.54
)) and also linear flows generated by para–differential operators.
The class of spectrally localized maps do not enjoy a symbolic calculus
and it is reminiscent of
the maps
introduced in Definition 1.2.1 in
[
37
]
.

The class of spectrally localized maps is closed under transposition (Lemma
2.18
)
and under “external” and “internal” compositions,
see Proposition
2.19
. A key property is that
the transpose of the internal differential of a spectrally localized map is a smoothing operator, see Lemma
2.21
.

Definition 2.16
.

(Spectrally localized maps)

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
,
p,N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
K,K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
K^{\prime}\leq K
and
r>0
.

(i)
Spectrally localized
p
-homogeneous maps.

We denote by

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}

the subspace of
m
-operators
S(\mathcal{U})
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{m}_{p}

satisfying the following spectral condition: there exist
\delta>0
,
C>1
such that for any

(U_{1},\ldots,U_{p})\in(\dot{H}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}^{2}))^{p}
, for any

U_{p+1}\in\dot{H}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C})
and for any
n_{0},\ldots,n_{p+1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\Pi_{n_{0}}S(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\ldots,\Pi_{n_{p}}U_{p})\Pi_{n_{p+1}}U_{p+1}\neq 0\,,

it results

\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{p}\}\leq\delta n_{p+1}\,,\qquad C^{-1}\,n_{0}\leq n_{p+1}\leq C\,n_{0}\,.

(2.71)

We denote
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{p}:=\bigcup_{m}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}

and by
\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q}
the class of pluri-homogeneous spectrally localized maps of the form

\sum_{q=p}^{N}S_{q}
with
S_{q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q}

and

\Sigma_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q}:=\bigcup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q}
.
For
p\geq N+1
we mean that the sum is empty.

(ii)
Non-homogeneous spectrally localized maps.

We denote
\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r]

the space of maps
(U,t,V)\mapsto S(U;t)V
defined on
B^{K^{\prime}}_{s_{0}}(I;r)\times I\times C^{0}_{*}(I,\dot{H}^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}))
for some
s_{0}>0
,
which are linear in the variable
V
and such that the following holds true.
For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
there are
C>0
and

r(s)\in]0,r[
such that for any

U\in B^{K}_{s_{0}}(I;r(s))\cap C^{K}_{*}(I,\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2}))
,
any
V\in C^{K-K^{\prime}}_{*}(I,\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}))
, any
0\leq k\leq K-K^{\prime}
,
t\in I
, we have that

\|{\partial_{t}^{k}\left(S(U;t)V\right)(t,\cdot)}\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k-m}}\leq C\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\|{U}\|_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{p}\|{V}\|_{k^{\prime\prime},s}\,.

(2.72)

In case
p=0
we require the estimate

\|{\partial_{t}^{k}\left(S(U;t)V\right)}\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k-m}}\leq C\|{V}\|_{k,s}
.

We denote
\mathcal{S}_{K,K^{\prime},N}[r]=\bigcup_{m}\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},N}[r]
.

(iii)
Spectrally localized Maps.

We denote by
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
,
the space of maps
(U,t,V)\to S(U;t)V
of the form

S(U;t)V=\sum_{q=p}^{N}S_{q}(U,\ldots,U)V+S_{>N}(U;t)V

(2.73)

where
S_{q}
are spectrally localized homogeneous maps in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q}
,
q=p,\ldots,N

and
S_{>N}
is a non–homogeneous spectrally localized map in

\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.
We denote
by
\Sigma\mathcal{S}_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}[r,N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

the space of
2\times 2
matrices whose entries are spectrally localized maps in

\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
.
We will use also the notation

\Sigma\mathcal{S}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]:=\bigcup_{m\geq 0}\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
.

\bullet

Note that (
2.71
) implies that

\max\{n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+1}\}\sim n_{p+1}
and

\max_{2}\{n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+1}\}\sim\max\{n_{1},\ldots,n_{p}\}

and therefore, by
(
2.31
),

S\in\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}
if and only if there are
\mu\geq 0
,
C>0
such that

\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\ldots,\Pi_{n_{p}}U_{p})\Pi_{n_{p+1}}U_{p+1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C{\rm\max}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p})^{\mu}\,n_{p+1}^{m}\prod_{j=1}^{p+1}\|\Pi_{n_{j}}U_{j}\|_{L^{2}}

(2.74)

for any
n_{0},\ldots,n_{p+1}\in\mathbb{N}
.

\bullet

In view of Lemma
2.9
a matrix of spectrally localized maps
S(U)
is characterized in terms of its Fourier coefficients as follows:
S(U)
is in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{p}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
if and only if
S_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
(defined as in (
2.43
)) satisfy,
for some
\mu\geq 0
,
C>0
,

|S_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}|\leq C{\rm max}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|\}^{\mu}|j|^{m}\,,\quad\forall(\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k)\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{p+2}\,,\ (\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma)\in\{\pm\}^{p+2}\,,

(2.75)

and if
S_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}\not=0
then (
2.44
), (
2.45
) hold and, for some
\delta>0
,

\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|\}\leq\delta|j|\,,\quad C^{-1}\,|k|\leq|j|\leq C\,|k|\,.

(2.76)

\bullet

If
S
is a
p
–homogeneous
spectrally localized map in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}
then

S(U)[V]
defines a non–homogeneous spectrally localized map in
\mathcal{S}_{K,0,p}^{m}[r]
for any
r>0
. The proof is similar to the one of Lemma
2.8
noting that the estimate (
2.72
) holds for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
because of the equivalence
n_{0}\sim n_{p+1}\sim\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{p+1}\}
in (
2.71
).

\bullet

(Paradifferential operators as spectrally localized maps)

If
a(U;t,x,\xi)
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]

then the paradifferential operator
{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}(a(U;t,x,\xi))
is a spectrally localized map in

\Sigma\mathcal{S}_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}[r,N]
. This is a consequence of Proposition 3.8 in
[
16
]
.
We remark that for a homogeneous symbol this is a consequence of the
choice of the first quantization in
(
2.23
).

\bullet

If
S(U,\dots,U)
is a
p
–homogeneous spectrally localized map in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{p}
, then the differential of the non–linear map
S(U,\dots,U)U
,
{\rm d}_{U}\big(S(U,\dots,U)U\big)V=pS(V,U,\dots,U)U+S(U,\dots,U)V
is a
p
–homogeneous operator in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{p}
,
not
necessarily spectrally localized. Indeed the operator
S(V,U,\dots,U)U
is
not
, in general, spectrally localized.

\bullet

If
m_{1}\leq m_{2}
then
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m_{1}}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]\subseteq\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m_{2}}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
.
If
K_{1}^{\prime}\leq K^{\prime}_{2}
then
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime}_{1},p}[r,N]\subseteq\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime}_{2},p}[r,N]
.

Remark 2.17
.

The constant
\delta>0
in the spectral condition (
2.71
) is not assumed to be small (unlike the one in (
2.25
) for paradifferential operators).

The class of matrices of spectrally localized homogeneous maps is closed under transposition.

Lemma 2.18
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
S(U)
is a matrix of
p
–homogeneous
spectrally localized maps in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
then

S(U)^{\top}
is in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, where the transpose is computed with respect to the non-degenerate real bilinear form (
2.4
).

Proof.

It results

|(S^{\top})_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{emmetop}}}{{=}}|S_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},-k,-j}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{smoocaraspec}}}{{\leq}}C{\rm max}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|\}^{\mu}|k|^{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{smoocaraspecres}}}{{\leq}}C^{\prime}{\rm max}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|\}^{\mu}|j|^{m}

which means that
(S^{\top})_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
satisfies (
2.75
). Since
|j|\sim|k|
then
\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|\}\leq\delta C|k|
hence (
2.76
) holds for
(S^{\top})_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
.
∎

We now prove some further composition results for spectrally localized maps.

Proposition 2.19
.

(Compositions of spectrally localized maps)

Let
p,p^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime},N,K,K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
K^{\prime}\leq K
and
r>0
.
Let
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
and
S(U;t)
be a spectrally localized map in
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
. Then

(i)

if
R(U;t)
is a smoothing operator in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime}}[r,N]
for some
\varrho\geq 0
, then
S(U;t)\circ R(U;t)
and
R(U;t)\circ S(U;t)

are smoothing operators in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+\max(m,0)}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime\prime}}[r,N]
.

(ii)

If
S^{\prime}(U;t)
is in
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime}}[r,N]
then the composition
S(U;t)\circ S^{\prime}(U;t)
is in
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m+m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[r,N]
.

(iii)

If
S
is in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}
and
S^{(a)}(U;t)
are spectrally localized maps in
\Sigma\mathcal{S}_{K,K^{\prime},q_{a}}^{\ell_{a}}[r,N]
for some
\ell_{a}\in\mathbb{R}
and
q_{a}\in\mathbb{N}
,
a=1,\dots,p
, then also the internal composition

S(S^{(1)}(U;t)U,\dots,S^{(p)}(U;t)U)

(2.77)

is a spectrally localized map in
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p+\overline{q}}[r,N]

with
\overline{q}:=\sum_{a=1}^{p}q_{a}
.

(iv)

If
S(U;t)
is in
{\mathcal{S}}_{K,0,p}^{m}[\breve{r}]
for any
\breve{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}

and
{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)
belongs to
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},0}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
,
then
S({\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U;t)
is in
\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r]
.

(v)

If
S(U)
is a matrix of

p
-homogeneous spectrally localized maps in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{p}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
and
S_{a}(U;t)
are in
\mathcal{S}^{m_{a}}_{K,K^{\prime},q_{a}}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
,
a=1,2
,
m_{a}\in\mathbb{R}
, then

{\rm d}_{U}\left.(S(U)U)\right|_{S_{1}(U;t)U}\,[S_{2}(U;t)U]

is in
\mathcal{S}^{m+\max(m_{1},m_{2})}_{K,K^{\prime},p+pq_{1}+q_{2}}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

Proof of
(i)
.
The operator
R(U;t)\circ S(U;t)

is in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+\max(m,0)}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime\prime}}[r,N]

by Proposition
2.15
-(
i
) since

S(U;t)
is in
\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
.
Then we prove that
S(U;t)\circ R(U;t)
is in
\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+\max(m,0)}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime\prime}}[r,N]
. It is sufficient to consider the case

m\geq 0
since, if
m<0
, we regard
S(U;t)
as a spectrally localized map in
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
. Decomposing
S=\sum_{q=p}^{N}S_{q}+S_{>N}
as in (
2.73
) and
R=\sum_{q=p^{\prime\prime}}^{N}R_{q}+R_{>N}
as in (
2.35
), we have to show, on the one hand that

S_{q_{1}}(U_{1},\dots,U_{q_{1}})R_{q_{2}}(U_{q_{1}+1},\dots,U_{q_{1}+q_{2}})

(2.78)

is a homogeneous smoothing operator in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho+m}_{q_{1}+q_{2}}
if
q_{1}+q_{2}\leq N
and, on the other hand, that

\displaystyle S_{q_{1}}(U,\dots,U)R_{q_{2}}(U,\dots,U),\ q_{1}+q_{2}\geq N+1,

(2.79)

\displaystyle S_{>N}(U;t)R_{q_{2}}(U,\dots,U),\ q_{2}=p^{\prime\prime},\dots,N,

\displaystyle S_{q_{1}}(U,\dots,U)R_{>N}(U;t),\ q_{1}=p,\dots,N,

\displaystyle S_{>N}(U;t)R_{>N}(U;t)

are non–homogeneous smoothing operators in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+m}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.
We first study (
2.78
). First of all one has

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S_{q_{1}}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}}}U_{q_{1}})R_{q_{2}}(\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+1}}U_{q_{1}+1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}})\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|_{L^{2}}

(2.80)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n^{\prime}}\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S_{q_{1}}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}}}U_{q_{1}})\Pi_{n^{\prime}}R_{q_{2}}(\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+1}}U_{q_{1}+1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}})\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|_{L^{2}}.

Thanks to the conditions (
2.32
), (
2.71
) for
S

and (
2.32
) for
R

the indices in the above sum satisfy,
for some choice of signs
\sigma_{a}
,
a=1,\dots,q_{1}+q_{2}+1
, the restrictions

n^{\prime}=\sum_{a=0}^{q_{1}}\sigma_{a}n_{a},\quad n^{\prime}=\sum_{a=q_{1}+1}^{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\sigma_{a}n_{a},\quad\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}}\}\lesssim n^{\prime},\quad n_{0}\sim n^{\prime}\,.

(2.81)

As a consequence (
2.78
) satisfies the corresponding condition (
2.32
) and

n^{\prime}\lesssim\max\{n_{q_{1}+1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\},\quad\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}\sim\max\{n_{q_{1}+1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}.

(2.82)

We also claim that

\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}}\}\lesssim{\rm max}_{2}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}.

(2.83)

Indeed, by (
2.81
) and (
2.82
), we have

\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}}\}\lesssim\max\{n_{q_{1}+1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\},

(2.84)

and we distinguish two cases. If
\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}=\max\{n_{q_{1}+1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}
then we directly obtain
\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}}\}\leq{\rm max}_{2}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}
which gives (
2.83
).
If
\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}=\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}}\}
then
\max\{n_{q_{1}+1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}\leq{\rm max}_{2}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}
which together with (
2.84
) proves (
2.83
).
Using (
2.80
), (
2.81
), (
2.74
), (
2.31
) (with
m=-\varrho
and
\mu\leadsto\mu^{\prime}
) we get

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n_{0}}

\displaystyle S_{q_{1}}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}}}U_{q_{1}})R_{q_{2}}(\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+1}}U_{q_{1}+1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}})\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C{\rm max}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}}\}^{\mu}(n^{\prime})^{m}\frac{\max_{2}\{n_{q_{1}+1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}^{\mu^{\prime}}}{\max\{n_{q_{1}+1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}^{\varrho}}\prod_{a=1}^{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{claimo},\eqref{denaco},m\geq 0}}{{\leq}}C\frac{\max_{2}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}^{\mu^{\prime}+\mu}}{\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\}^{\varrho-m}}\prod_{a=1}^{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|_{L^{2}}.

This proves that (
2.78
) is in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho+m}_{q_{1}+q_{2}}
.

In order to prove that the operators in (
2.79
) satisfy (
2.34
) (with
m\leadsto-\varrho+m
and
p\leadsto N
) we recall that
S_{q_{1}}(U,\dots,U)
defines a spectrally localized map in
\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,0,q_{1}}[r]
(as remarked below Definition
2.16
) and
R_{q_{2}}(U,\dots,U)
defines a smoothing operator in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,q_{2}}[r]
thanks to Lemma
2.8
.
Then the thesis follows by estimates (
2.72
) and (
2.34
). For instance consider the last term in (
2.79
).
For any

k=0,\ldots,K-K^{\prime}

\displaystyle\|{\partial_{t}^{k}\left(S_{>N}(U;t)R_{>N}(U;t)V\right)}\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k-m+\varrho}}\leq C\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\|U\|_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{N+1}\|R_{>N}(U;t)V\|_{k^{\prime\prime},s+\varrho}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\sum_{0\leq j\leq k^{\prime\prime}\atop j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}=j}\|U\|_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{N+1}\,\left(\|{V}\|_{j^{\prime\prime},s}\|U\|_{j^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{N+1}+\|{V}\|_{j^{\prime\prime},s_{0}}\|U\|_{j^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{N}\|U\|_{j^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}=0}^{k}\|{V}\|_{j^{\prime\prime},s}\|U\|_{k-j^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{2N+2}+\|{V}\|_{j^{\prime\prime},s_{0}}\|U\|_{k-j^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{2N+1}\|U\|_{k-j^{\prime\prime}+K^{\prime},s}

using that
k^{\prime}\leq k-j^{\prime\prime}
and
j^{\prime}\leq k-j^{\prime\prime}
. This proves that (
2.78
)
is in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+m}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
(as
\|U\|_{K,s_{0}}<1
).

Proof of
(ii)
.

Decomposing
S=\sum_{q=p}^{N}S_{q}+S_{>N}
and
S^{\prime}=\sum_{q=p^{\prime}}^{N}S^{\prime}_{q}+S^{\prime}_{>N}
as in (
2.73
), we have to show, on the one hand that

S_{q_{1}}(U_{1},\dots,U_{q_{1}})S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(U_{q_{1}+1},\dots,U_{q_{1}+q_{2}})

(2.85)

is a homogeneous spectrally localized map in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}^{m+m^{\prime}}_{q_{1}+q_{2}}
if
q_{1}+q_{2}\leq N
and, on the other hand, that

\displaystyle S_{q_{1}}(U,\dots,U)S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(U,\dots,U),\ q_{1}+q_{2}\geq N+1,

(2.86)

\displaystyle S_{>N}(U;t)S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(U,\dots,U),\ q_{2}=p^{\prime},\dots,N,

\displaystyle S_{q_{1}}(U,\dots,U)S^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t),\ q_{1}=p,\dots,N,

\displaystyle S_{>N}(U;t)S^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t)

are non–homogeneous spectrally localized map in
\mathcal{S}^{m+m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.
We first prove that (
2.85
) is in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}^{m+m^{\prime}}_{q_{1}+q_{2}}
. First of all one has

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S_{q_{1}}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}}}U_{q_{1}})S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+1}}U_{q_{1}+1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}})\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|_{L^{2}}

(2.87)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n^{\prime}}\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S_{q_{1}}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}}}U_{q_{1}})\Pi_{n^{\prime}}S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+1}}U_{q_{1}+1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}})\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|_{L^{2}}.

Thanks to the conditions (
2.32
), (
2.71
) for
S
and
S^{\prime}
the indices in the above sum satisfy, for some choice of signs
\sigma_{a}
,
a=1,\dots,q_{1}+q_{2}+1
, the restriction

\displaystyle

\displaystyle n^{\prime}=\sum_{a=0}^{q_{1}}\sigma_{a}n_{a},\quad n^{\prime}=\sum_{a=q_{1}+1}^{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\sigma_{a}n_{a},

(2.88)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}}\}\leq\delta n^{\prime}\\
\frac{n_{0}}{C}\leq n^{\prime}\leq Cn_{0}\,,\end{cases}\quad\begin{cases}\max\{n_{q_{1}+1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}\}\leq\delta^{\prime}n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\\
\frac{n^{\prime}}{C^{\prime}}\leq n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\leq C^{\prime}n^{\prime}\,,\end{cases}

for some
C,C^{\prime}>1
and
\delta,\delta^{\prime}>0
.
Therefore

\begin{cases}\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}\}\leq\max\{\delta^{\prime},\delta C^{\prime}\}n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\\
\frac{n_{0}}{CC^{\prime}}\leq n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\leq CC^{\prime}n_{0}\,.\end{cases}

This proves that
S_{q_{1}}\circ S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}
fulfills the localization property of Definition
2.16

(i)
(see (
2.71
)).
In addition (
2.85
) satisfies the corresponding condition (
2.32
). Using (
2.87
), (
2.74
) we get

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S_{q_{1}}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}}}U_{q_{1}})S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+1}}U_{q_{1}+1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}})\Pi_{n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}}U_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C{\rm max}\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}}\}^{\mu}(n^{\prime})^{m}{\rm max}\{n_{q_{1}+1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}\}^{\mu^{\prime}}n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}^{m^{\prime}}\prod_{a=1}^{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{denaco3}}}{{\leq}}C\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q_{1}+q_{2}}\}^{\mu^{\prime}+\mu}(n_{q_{1}+q_{2}+1})^{m+m^{\prime}}\prod_{a=1}^{q_{1}+q_{2}+1}\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|_{L^{2}}

which proves that
S_{q_{1}}\circ S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}
satisfies (
2.74
).
In order to prove that the terms in (
2.79
) satisfy (
2.72
) we first note that, thanks to
(i)
of Lemma
2.18
, we have that
S_{q_{1}}(U,\dots,U)\in\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,0,q_{1}}[r]
and
S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(U,\dots,U)\in\mathcal{S}^{m^{\prime}}_{K,0,q_{2}}[r]
and
then the thesis follows using (
2.72
).
For instance consider the first term in (
2.86
).
Using twice (
2.72
) (with
K^{\prime}=0
) we get, for any
k=0,\ldots,K
,

\displaystyle\|{\partial_{t}^{k}\left(S_{q_{1}}(U;t)S^{\prime}_{q_{2}}(U;t)V\right)}\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k-m_{1}-m_{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\|U\|_{k^{\prime},s_{0}}^{q_{1}}\|S_{q_{2}}^{\prime}(U;t)V\|_{k^{\prime\prime},s-m_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\sum_{0\leq j\leq k^{\prime\prime}\atop j^{\prime}+j^{\prime\prime}=j}\|U\|_{k^{\prime},s_{0}}^{q_{1}}\,\|U\|_{j^{\prime},s_{0}}^{q_{2}}\,\|{V}\|_{j^{\prime\prime},s}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}=0}^{k}\|U\|_{k-j^{\prime\prime},s_{0}}^{q_{1}+q_{2}}\|{V}\|_{j^{\prime\prime},s}

where in the last step we used that

j^{\prime}\leq k-j^{\prime\prime}
and
k^{\prime}\leq k-j^{\prime\prime}
.
The last line is
(
2.72
)
with
p
replaced by
q_{1}+q_{2}\geq N+1
.

Proof of
(iii)
:
We now consider the internal composition (
2.77
) in the homogeneous case.
For simplicity of notation we consider the case
q_{2}=\dots=q_{p}=0
,
S^{(2)}=\dots=S^{(p)}={\rm Id}
and
q_{1}=\overline{q}=:q
, the general case follows in the same way.
So we need to show that

S(S^{(1)}(U)U,U,\ldots,U)

is a spectrally localized map in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p+q}
. We first estimate

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S\big(S^{(1)}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q}}U_{q})\Pi_{n_{q+1}}U_{q+1},\Pi_{n_{q+2}}U_{q+2},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q+p}}U_{q+p}\big)\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}U_{p+q+1}\|_{L^{2}}

(2.89)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n^{\prime}}\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S\big(\Pi_{n^{\prime}}S^{(1)}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q}}U_{q})\Pi_{n_{q+1}}U_{q+1},\Pi_{n_{q+2}}U_{q+2},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q+p}}U_{q+p}\big)\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}U_{p+q+1}\|_{L^{2}}.

Thanks to the conditions (
2.32
) for
S
and
S^{(1)}
the indices in the above sum satisfy, for some choice of signs
\sigma_{a}
,
a=1,\dots,p+q+1
, the restrictions

n^{\prime}=\sum_{a=1}^{q+1}\sigma_{a}n_{a}
and
n^{\prime}=\sigma_{0}n_{0}+\sum_{a=q+2}^{p+q+1}\sigma_{a}n_{a}
,
proving that
S(S^{(1)}(U)U,U,\ldots,U)
fulfills (
2.32
).
Moreover the condition (
2.71
) for
S
and
S^{(1)}
imply the existence of
\delta,\delta_{1}>0
,
C,C_{1}>1
such that

\begin{cases}\max\{n^{\prime},n_{q+2},\dots,n_{q+p}\}\leq\delta n_{q+p+1}\\
\frac{n_{0}}{C}\leq n_{p+q+1}\leq Cn_{0}\,,\end{cases}\quad\begin{cases}\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{q}\}\leq\delta_{1}n_{q+1}\\
\frac{n^{\prime}}{C_{1}}\leq n_{q+1}\leq C_{1}n^{\prime}\,,\end{cases}

(2.90)

and therefore

\max\{n_{1},\dots,n_{p+q}\}\leq\max(\delta\delta_{1}C_{1},\delta C_{1})n_{q+p+1}
and
\frac{n_{0}}{C}\leq n_{p+q+1}\leq Cn_{0}
,
proving that
S(S^{(1)}(U)U,U,\ldots,U)
fulfills (
2.71
).
We now prove it fulfills also (
2.74
).
We get

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S(S^{(1)}(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q}}U_{q})\Pi_{n_{q+1}}U_{q+1},\Pi_{n_{q+2}}U_{q+2},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q+p}}U_{q+p})\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}U_{p+q+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{triangolo4},\eqref{hom:sest}}}{{\lesssim}}\sum_{n^{\prime}}\max(n^{\prime},n_{q+2},\ldots,n_{q+p})^{\mu}\,n_{p+q+1}^{m}\,\max(n_{1},\ldots,n_{q})^{\mu^{\prime}}\,n_{q+1}^{\ell_{1}}\,\prod_{a=1}^{p+q+1}\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{doppiacondizione},n_{q+1}\sim n^{\prime}}}{{\lesssim}}\sum_{n^{\prime}}\max(n_{q+1},n_{q+2},\ldots,n_{q+p})^{\mu+\max(0,\ell_{1})}\,n_{p+q+1}^{m}\,\max(n_{1},\ldots,n_{q})^{\mu^{\prime}}\,\prod_{a=1}^{p+q+1}\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+q})^{\mu+\mu^{\prime}+\max(0,\ell_{1})}\,n_{p+q+1}^{m}\,\prod_{a=1}^{p+q+1}\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|_{L^{2}}\ ,

proving that

S(S^{(1)}(U)U,U,\ldots,U)

is a spectrally localized map in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p+q}
.
Finally
S(S^{(1)}(U)U,U,\ldots,U)
satisfies also (
2.33
), concluding the proof that it
is a spectrally localized map in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p+q}
.

Proof of
(iv)
:
By the estimate below (
2.34
) for
{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)
, for any
U\in B_{s_{0}}^{K}(I;r)
and any
k=0,\dots,K-K^{\prime}
,
\|{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U\|_{k,s_{0}}\lesssim\|U\|_{k,s_{0}}
. Then estimate (
2.72
) for
S({\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U;t)
for any
0\leq k\leq K-K^{\prime}
follows from the ones for
S(U;t)
arguing as in
(iii)
of Proposition
2.15
.

Proof of
(v)
:
It follows computing explicitly the differential
{\rm d}_{U}(S(U)U)[V]
, evaluating it at
U\leadsto S_{1}(U;t)U
and
V\leadsto S_{2}(U;t)U
and using item
(ii)
and
(iii)
of the proposition.
∎

The following lemma proves that the internal composition
of a spectrally localized map with a map, is a spectrally localized map
plus a smoothing operator whose transpose is another smoothing operator.

Lemma 2.20
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
and
m^{\prime}\geq 0
.
Let
S(U)
be a matrix of spectrally localized homogeneous maps in

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{p}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
and
M(U)
be a matrix of
homogeneous
m^{\prime}
-operators in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{q}^{m^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
. Then

S(M(U)U,U,\dots,U)=S^{\prime}(U)+R(U)

(2.91)

where

•

S^{\prime}(U)
is a matrix of spectrally localized homogeneous maps in

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{p+q}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
;

•

R(U)
is a matrix of homogeneous smoothing operators in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p+q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\varrho\geq 0
,
and
R(U)^{\top}
is a matrix of homogeneous smoothing operators
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p+q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\varrho\geq 0
as well.

Proof.

By multilinearity we expand

\displaystyle S\big(M(U_{1},\dots,U_{q})U_{q+1},U_{q+2},\dots,U_{p+q}\big)

(2.92)

\displaystyle=\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(n^{\prime},\,n_{0},\,\dots,n_{p+q+1})\in\mathcal{N}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\Pi_{n_{0}}S\big(\Pi_{n^{\prime}}M(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q}}U_{q})\Pi_{n_{q+1}}U_{q+1},\Pi_{n_{q+2}}U_{q+2},\dots,\Pi_{n_{p+q}}U_{p+q}\big)\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}

where
\mathcal{N}
is a subset of
\mathbb{N}^{p+q+3}
made by indexes fulfilling, by restrictions (
2.71
) and (
2.32
), the following conditions: there exist
signs
\{\sigma_{j},\epsilon_{j},\epsilon^{\prime}\}\subset\{\pm\}

and constants
\delta>0
,
C>1
such that

\begin{cases}\max(n^{\prime},n_{q+2},\dots,n_{p+q})\leq\delta n_{p+q+1}&(a)\\
\quad C^{-1}n_{0}\leq n_{p+q+1}\leq Cn_{0}&(b)\\
n^{\prime}=\sum_{j=1}^{q+1}\sigma_{j}n_{j},\quad n_{0}=\epsilon^{\prime}n^{\prime}+\sum_{j=q+2}^{p+q+1}\epsilon_{j}n_{j}\,&(c)\ .\end{cases}

(2.93)

We fix
\delta^{\prime}>\delta
and we denote by
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
the subset of
\mathcal{N}
made by indices which satisfy the additional restriction

\max(n_{1},\dots,n_{q+1})\leq\delta^{\prime}n_{p+q+1}\,.

(2.94)

Then we define

\displaystyle S^{\prime}(U_{1},\dots,U_{p+q})

(2.95)

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(n^{\prime},\,n_{0},\,\dots,n_{p+q+1})\in\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\Pi_{n_{0}}S\big(\Pi_{n^{\prime}}M(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q}}U_{q})\Pi_{n_{q+1}}U_{q+1},\Pi_{n_{q+2}}U_{q+2},\dots,\Pi_{n_{p+q}}U_{p+q}\big)\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}.

By (
2.93
) and (
2.94
) one has that
S^{\prime}(U)
fulfills
the spectral condition

n_{0}\sim n_{p+q+1}
,
\max(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+q})\leq\delta^{\prime}n_{p+q+1}

which is the condition (
2.71
).
Moreover using (
2.74
) and (
2.31
) we bound

\displaystyle\Big\|\Pi_{n_{0}}S\big(\Pi_{n^{\prime}}M(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q}}U_{q})\Pi_{n_{q+1}}U_{q+1},\Pi_{n_{q+2}}U_{q+2},\dots,\Pi_{n_{p+q}}U_{p+q}\big)\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}U_{p+q+q}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+q})^{\mu+m^{\prime}}\,n_{p+q+1}^{m}\,\prod_{a=1}^{p+q+1}{\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|}_{L^{2}}

(2.96)

for some
\mu>0
.
Finally by (
2.93
)
(c)
one has also that (
2.32
) holds.
One checks that also (
2.33
) holds true.
We have proved that
S^{\prime}(U)
is a matrix of spectrally localized maps in

\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p+q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Then, recalling (
2.92
) and (
2.95
), we define

\displaystyle R(U_{1},\dots,U_{p+q})

(2.97)

\displaystyle:=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(n^{\prime},\,n_{0},\,\dots,n_{p+q+1})\in\mathcal{N}\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\Pi_{n_{0}}S\big(\Pi_{n^{\prime}}M(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\dots,\Pi_{n_{q}}U_{q})\Pi_{n_{q+1}}U_{q+1},\Pi_{n_{q+2}}U_{q+2},\dots,\Pi_{n_{p+q}}U_{p+q}\big)\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}.

We claim that there is
C^{\prime}>0
such that
if
(n^{\prime},\,n_{0},\,\dots,n_{p+q+1})\in\mathcal{N}\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
then

\begin{cases}\max(n_{q+2},\dots,n_{p+q})\leq\delta^{\prime}n_{p+q+1}&(a)\\
C^{-1}n_{0}\leq n_{p+q+1}\leq Cn_{0}&(b)\\
n_{p+q+1}\leq C^{\prime}\max_{2}(n_{1},\dots,n_{q+1})&(c)\\
\max(n_{1},\dots,n_{q+1})\leq C^{\prime}\max_{2}(n_{1},\dots,n_{q+1})&(d)\,.\end{cases}

(2.98)

Before proving (
2.98
) we note that it implies

\displaystyle\max(n_{1},\dots,n_{p+q+1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}(1+\delta^{\prime})\max(n_{1},\dots,n_{q+1},n_{p+q+1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)+(d)}}{{\leq}}(1+\delta^{\prime})C^{\prime}{\rm max}_{2}(n_{1},\dots,n_{q+1})\leq(1+\delta^{\prime})C^{\prime}{\rm max}_{2}(n_{1},\dots,n_{p+q+1})

proving that

\max(n_{1},\dots,n_{p+q+1})\sim{\rm max}_{2}(n_{1},\dots,n_{p+q+1})
.
Then by (
2.97
) and (
2.96
) we obtain

\displaystyle{\|\Pi_{n_{0}}R(\Pi_{n_{1}}U_{1},\ldots,\Pi_{n_{p+q}}U_{p+q})\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}U_{p+q+1}\|}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\max(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+q+1})^{\widetilde{\mu}}\,\prod_{a=1}^{p+q+1}{\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\max_{2}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+q+1})^{\widetilde{\mu}+\varrho}}{\max(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+q+1})^{\varrho}}\prod_{a=1}^{p+q+1}{\|\Pi_{n_{a}}U_{a}\|}_{L^{2}}\qquad\text{with}\qquad\widetilde{\mu}=\mu+m^{\prime}+\max(m,0)\,,

showing that

R(U)
is a
(p+q)
–homogeneous smoothing operator in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{p+q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\varrho\geq 0
.

We now prove (
2.98
). Note that
(a)
and
(b)
of (
2.98
) follow by
(a)
and
(b)
of (
2.93
) and
\delta^{\prime}>\delta
. Then note that if
(n^{\prime},\,n_{0},\,\dots,n_{p+q+1})\in\mathcal{N}\setminus\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
then

\max(n_{1},\dots,n_{q+1})>\delta^{\prime}n_{p+q+1}\,.

(2.99)

Then, by
(c)
of (
2.93
), one has

\max(n_{1},\ldots,n_{q+1})=\epsilon^{\prime}n^{\prime}+\sum_{n_{j}\leq{\rm\max}_{2}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{q+1})}\epsilon_{j}n_{j}\leq n^{\prime}+q{\rm\max}_{2}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{q+1})

(2.100)

so that

{\rm max}_{2}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{q+1})\geq\frac{1}{q}\big(\max(n_{1},\ldots,n_{q+1})-n^{\prime}\big)
.
We deduce
using (
2.99
) and (
2.93
)
(a)
that

{\rm\max}_{2}(n_{1},\ldots,n_{q+1})\geq\frac{\delta^{\prime}-\delta}{q}\ n_{p+q+1}

(2.101)

thus proving (
2.98
)
(c)
.
Finally using (
2.100
), (
2.93
) (a) and (
2.101
) we get

\max(n_{1},\dots,n_{q+1})\leq q\big(\frac{\delta}{\delta^{\prime}-\delta}+1\big){\rm max}_{2}(n_{1},\dots,n_{q+1})

which proves
(d)
of (
2.98
).

We finally prove that
R(U)^{\top}
is a smoothing operator. First note that if
\Pi_{n_{0}}R(\Pi_{\vec{n}}\mathcal{U})^{\top}\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}\neq 0
then
\Pi_{n_{p+q+1}}R(\Pi_{\vec{n}}\mathcal{U})\Pi_{n_{0}}\neq 0
, which implies that

C^{-1}n_{p+q+1}\leq n_{0}\leq Cn_{p+q+1}
by (
2.98
)
(b)
.
Then
R(U)^{\top}
is a smoothing operator in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{p+q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

for any
\varrho\geq 0
by Lemma
2.10
and Remark
2.11
.
∎

We finally prove the following lemma
which generalizes a result in
[
45
]
for paradifferential operators:
the transpose of the internal differential of a spectrally
localized map is a smoothing operator (with two equivalent frequencies).

Lemma 2.21
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
m\in\mathbb{R}
.
Given a matrix of spectrally localized
p
–homogeneous maps

S(U)\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{p}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, consider

V\mapsto L(U)V:=\frac{1}{p}{\rm d}_{U}S(U)[V]U=S(V,U,\dots,U)U\,\,.

(2.102)

Then the transposed map
L(U)^{\top}
is a matrix of smoothing operators in

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\varrho\geq 0
.

Proof.

We first note that the matrix entries
L(U)^{\top}
defined as in (
2.43
) are

\displaystyle[L^{\top}]^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{emmetop}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle L^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},-k,-j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{M.coeff}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}L\Big(\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{1}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{1}j_{1}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},\dots,\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{p}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{p}j_{p}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big)\Big[\frac{\mathtt{q}^{\sigma}e^{-{\rm i}\sigma kx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big]\cdot\mathtt{q}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma^{\prime}jx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{effonetra}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}S\Big(\mathtt{q}^{\sigma}\frac{e^{-{\rm i}\sigma kx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{1}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{1}j_{1}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\dots,\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{p-1}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{p-1}j_{p-1}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big)\Big[\frac{\mathtt{q}^{\sigma_{p}}e^{{\rm i}\sigma_{p}j_{p}x}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\Big]\cdot\mathtt{q}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\frac{e^{{\rm i}\sigma^{\prime}jx}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,{\rm d}x

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{M.coeff}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle S^{\sigma,\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{p-1},\sigma_{p},\sigma^{\prime}}_{-k,j_{1},\dots,j_{p-1},j_{p},-j}\,

(2.103)

which, by (
2.76
), are different from zero only if

{\rm max}\{|k|,|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p-1}|\}\leq\delta|j_{p}|\,,\quad C^{-1}|j|\leq|j_{p}|\leq C|j|\,.

(2.104)

The restriction (
2.104
) implies that

\max(|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|)\sim{\rm max}_{2}(|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|)

(2.105)

because

\displaystyle\max(|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta)\max(|j_{p}|,|j|)

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\delta){\rm max}_{2}(|j_{p}|,|j|)\leq C(1+\delta){\rm max}_{2}(|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|)\,.

Finally by (
2.103
), we estimate

\displaystyle\big|[L^{\top}]^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}\big|=\big|S^{\sigma,\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{p-1},\sigma_{p},\sigma^{\prime}}_{-k,j_{1},\dots,j_{p-1},j_{p},-j}\big|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{smoocaraspec}}}{{\leq}}C{\rm max}\{|k|,|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p-1}|\}^{\mu}|j_{p}|^{m}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{altrospec}}}{{\leq}}C^{\prime}{\rm max}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\mu+\max(m,0)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{segunda}}}{{\leq}}C^{\prime\prime}\frac{{\rm max}_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\mu+\max(m,0)+\varrho}}{{\rm max}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\varrho}}

implying, in view of Lemma
2.9
(with
m\leadsto-\varrho
and
\mu\leadsto\mu+\max(m,0)+\varrho
), that
L(U)^{\top}
is a matrix of smoothing operators in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\varrho\geq 0
.
∎

We conclude this section with a lemma which shall be used in Section
3.4
.

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varrho\geq 0
.
Let
S(U)
be a matrix of
p
-homogeneous
spectrally localized maps in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

of the form

S(U)=L(U)+R(U)\,,

(2.106)

where
L(U)^{\top}
and
R(U)
are matrices
of
p
-homogeneous smoothing
operators in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
Then
S(U)
is a matrix of
p
–homogeneous smoothing
operators in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

In view of
(
2.47
)
and (
2.48
) (for
L(U)^{\top}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
),

|L_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}|=|(L^{\top})_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},-k,-j}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}}|\leq C{\rm max}_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|k|\}^{\mu}\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|k|\}^{-\varrho}\ .

(2.107)

Since
S(U)
is spectrally localized,
its non zero Fourier coefficients

S_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}
(defined as in (
2.43
))
satisfy
|j|\sim|k|
(see (
2.76
))
and,
in view of (
2.106
),

\displaystyle|S_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}|\leq|L_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}|+|R_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p},\sigma^{\prime},\sigma}|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{L.t.s},\eqref{smoocara}}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{{\rm max}_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|k|\}^{\mu}}{\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|k|\}^{\varrho}}+\frac{{\rm max}_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\mu}}{\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\varrho}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle|j|\sim|k|}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{{\rm max}_{2}\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\mu}}{\max\{|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{p}|,|j|\}^{\varrho}}

proving that
S(U)
is a smoothing operator in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
∎

2.3
Approximate inverse of non–linear maps
and flows

In this section we construct an approximate version of two fundamental non–linear operations that we will need: the inverse of a non–linear map and the flow generated by a non–linear vector field. We first provide
the definition of an approximate inverse of a map, up to homogeneity
N
.

Definition 2.23
.

(Approximate inverse up to homogeneity
N
)

Let
p,N\in\mathbb{N}
with
p\leq N
. Consider

\Psi_{\leq N}(U)=U+M_{\leq N}(U)U\quad\text{where}\quad M_{\leq N}(U)\in\Sigma_{p}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

(2.108)

is a matrix of pluri–homogeneous operators. We say that

\Phi_{\leq N}(V)=V+\breve{M}_{\leq N}(V)V\quad\text{where}\quad\breve{M}_{\leq N}(V)\in\Sigma_{p}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,,

(2.109)

is an approximate inverse of
\Psi_{\leq N}(U)

up to homogeneity
N
if

\displaystyle\big({\rm Id}+M_{\leq N}(\Phi_{{\leq N}}(V))\big)\big({\rm Id}+\breve{M}_{\leq N}(V)\big)={\rm Id}+M^{\prime}_{>N}(V)

(2.110)

\displaystyle\big({\rm Id}+\breve{M}_{\leq N}(\Psi_{{\leq N}}(U))\big)\big({\rm Id}+M_{\leq N}(U)\big)={\rm Id}+M^{\prime\prime}_{>N}(U)

where
M^{\prime}_{>N}(V)
and
M^{\prime\prime}_{>N}(U)
are pluri–homogeneous matrices of
operators in
\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Note that, if
\Phi_{\leq N}(V)
is an approximate inverse up to homogeneity
N
of
\Psi_{\leq N}(U)
then

\Psi_{\leq N}\circ\Phi_{{\leq N}}(V)=V+M^{\prime}_{>N}(V)V\,,\quad\Phi_{\leq N}\circ\Psi_{{\leq N}}(U)=U+M^{\prime\prime}_{>N}(U)U\,,

(2.111)

and, by differentiation and taking the transpose

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}_{U}\Psi_{\leq N}(\Phi_{{\leq N}}(V)){\rm d}_{V}\Phi_{{\leq N}}(V)={\rm Id}+M^{1}_{>N}(V)

(2.112)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}_{V}\Phi_{{\leq N}}(\Psi_{\leq N}(U)){\rm d}_{U}\Psi_{\leq N}(U)={\rm Id}+M^{2}_{>N}(U)\,

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}_{V}\Phi_{\leq N}(V)^{\top}\,{\rm d}_{U}\Psi_{\leq N}(\Phi_{\leq N}(V))^{\top}={\rm Id}+M^{3}_{>N}(V)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle d}_{U}\Psi_{\leq N}(\Phi_{\leq N}(V))^{\top}\,{\rm d}_{V}\Phi_{\leq N}(V)^{\top}={\rm Id}+M^{4}_{>N}(V)

where
M^{a}_{>N}(V)
,
a=1,\ldots,4

are other
pluri-homogeneous operators in
\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
(the differential of a homogeneous
m
-operator is a homogeneous
m
-operator by the first remark after Definition
2.5
, so is its transpose by Lemma
2.10
).

The following lemma ensures the existence of an approximate inverse.

Lemma 2.24
.

(Approximate inverse)

Let
p,N\in\mathbb{N}
with
p\leq N
. Consider
\Psi_{\leq N}(U)=U+M_{\leq N}(U)U
as in
(
2.108
).
Then there exists an approximate inverse of
\Psi_{\leq N}(U)
up to homogeneity
N

(according to Definition
2.23
) of the form (
2.109
) with

\mathcal{P}_{p}[\breve{M}_{\leq N}(V)]=-\mathcal{P}_{p}[M_{\leq N}(V)]\,.

(2.113)

Moreover, if
M_{\leq N}(U)
in (
2.108
) is a matrix of pluri–homogeneous

(i)

spectrally localized maps in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{q}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
,
m\geq 0
, then
\breve{M}_{\leq N}
in (
2.109
) is a matrix of pluri-homogeneous spectrally localized maps in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{q}^{m(N-p+1)}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

and
M^{\prime}_{>N}
,
M^{\prime\prime}_{>N}
in (
2.110
) belong to

\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{q}^{m(N-p+2)}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
;

(ii)

smoothing operators in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for some
\varrho\geq 0
, then

\breve{M}_{\leq N}
in (
2.109
) is a matrix of pluri-homogeneous smoothing operators
in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

and
M^{\prime}_{>N}
,
M^{\prime\prime}_{>N}
in (
2.110
) belong to
\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

We expand in homogeneous components

M(U):={\rm Id}+M_{\leq N}(U)={\rm Id}+\sum_{q=p}^{N}M_{q}(U)\quad\text{with}\quad M_{q}(U)\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,.

(2.114)

In order to solve
the first equation in (
2.110
)
we look for a pluri–homogeneous operator

\breve{M}(V)={\rm Id}+\breve{M}_{\leq N}(V)=\breve{M}_{0}(V)+\sum_{a=p}^{N}\breve{M}_{a}(V)\quad\text{with}\quad\breve{M}_{0}(V):={\rm Id}\,,\ \breve{M}_{a}(V)\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,,

(2.115)

such that

M\big(\breve{M}(V)V\big)\breve{M}(V)={\rm Id}+M_{>N}(V)\quad\text{with}\quad M_{>N}(V)\in\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,.

(2.116)

By (
2.114
), (
2.115
) we get

\displaystyle M\big(\breve{M}(V)V\big)\breve{M}(V)

\displaystyle={\rm Id}+\sum_{\ell=p}^{N}\breve{M}_{\ell}(V)+\sum_{q=p}^{N}\sum_{0\leq a_{1},\dots a_{q+1}\leq N}M_{q}(\breve{M}_{a_{1}}(V)V,\dots,\breve{M}_{a_{q}}(V)V)\breve{M}_{a_{q+1}}(V)

\displaystyle={\rm Id}+\sum_{\ell=p}^{N}\Big[\breve{M}_{\ell}(V)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\leq q\leq\ell\\
q+a_{1}+\dots+a_{q+1}=\ell\end{subarray}}M_{q}(\breve{M}_{a_{1}}(V)V,\dots,\breve{M}_{a_{q}}(V)V)\breve{M}_{a_{q+1}}(V)\Big]+M_{>N}(V)

and therefore equation (
2.116
) is recursively solved
by defining, for any

\ell=p,\dots,N,

\breve{M}_{\ell}(V,\dots,V):=-\sum_{q+a_{1}+\dots+a_{q+1}=\ell}M_{q}(\breve{M}_{a_{1}}(V)V,\dots,\breve{M}_{a_{q}}(V)V)\breve{M}_{a_{q+1}}(V)\,.

(2.117)

Note that each
\breve{M}_{\ell}(V,\dots,V)
is a matrix of homogeneous operators by
(ii)
of Proposition
2.15
.
We proved the first identity in (
2.110
).

We now prove the second identity in (
2.110
). Using the same recursive procedure
we find a matrix of pluri–homogeneous operators of the form

M^{\prime\prime}(U)={\rm Id}+\sum_{n=p}^{N}M^{\prime\prime}_{n}(U)\quad\text{with}\quad M^{\prime\prime}_{n}(U)\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

(2.118)

such that
M^{\prime\prime}(U)
is an approximate right inverse of
\breve{M}(V)
, i.e.

\breve{M}\big(M^{\prime\prime}(U)U\big)M^{\prime\prime}(U)={\rm Id}+M_{>N}(U)\quad\text{with}\quad M_{>N}(U)\in\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,.

(2.119)

Applying (
2.116
) with
V=M^{\prime\prime}(U)U
and right–composing it with
M^{\prime\prime}(U)
defined in
(
2.118
), we obtain by (
2.119
) and Proposition
2.15
that

M\big(U+M_{>N}(U)U\big)=M^{\prime\prime}(U)+M^{\prime}_{>N}(U)

(2.120)

where

M_{>N}(U)
and
M^{\prime}_{>N}(U)
are
operators in

\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
. Then, expanding
the left hand side of
(
2.120
)
by multilinearity, we get

M\big(U\big)-M^{\prime\prime}(U)=\sum_{q=p}^{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{q}\binom{q}{\ell}M_{q}(\underbrace{M_{>N}(U)U,\dots,M_{>N}(U)U}_{\ell-times},U,\dots,U)+M^{\prime}_{>N}(U)=\tilde{M}_{>N}(U)

where
\tilde{M}_{>N}(U)
is in
\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
thanks to
(ii)
of Proposition
2.15
. This implies, since both
M(U)
and
M^{\prime\prime}(U)
are pluri–homogeneous operators up to homogeneity
N
(cfr. (
2.114
), (
2.118
)),
that
M\big(U\big)=M^{\prime\prime}(U)
and, by (
2.119
), we conclude that

\breve{M}\big(M(U)U\big)M(U)={\rm Id}+M_{>N}(U)\,.

(2.121)

This proves, recalling the notation (
2.114
), (
2.115
), (
2.108
),
the second identity in (
2.110
).
Moreover for
\ell=p
the sum in (
2.117
) reduces to the unique element with
q=p
,
a_{1}=\dots=a_{q+1}=0
and
\breve{M}_{p}(V)=-M_{p}(V)
, proving (
2.113
).

(i)
If
M_{\leq N}(U)
is a spectrally localized map in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}^{m}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
we claim that
\breve{M}_{\ell}(V)
is a spectrally localized map in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}^{m+m(\ell-p)}_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for
\ell=p,\dots,N
. For
\ell=p

by (
2.113
) we have
\breve{M}_{p}(V)=-M_{p}(V)

which is in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}^{m}_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
Then supposing inductively that
\breve{M}_{a}(V)
is in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}^{m+m(a-p)}_{a}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for
a=p,\dots\ell-1
, we deduce
by
(ii)
and
(iii)
of Proposition
2.19
, that each term in the sum in (
2.117
) is a
spectrally localized map in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}^{m+(m+m(a_{q+1}-p))}_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
which is included in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}^{m+m(\ell-p)}_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
using that
a_{q+1}\leq\ell-1
.

(ii)
In the same way, if
M_{\leq N}(U)
is a smoothing operator in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}_{q}^{-\varrho}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, thanks to
(ii)
of Proposition
2.15
one proves recursively that
\breve{M}_{\ell}(V)
are smoothing
operators in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}_{\ell}^{-\varrho}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
∎

We now define the approximate flow of a smoothing

\tau
–dependent vector field.

Definition 2.25
.

(Approximate flow of a smoothing vector field up to homogeneity
N
)

Let
p,N\in\mathbb{N}
with
p\leq N
.
An
approximate flow up to homogeneity
N
of a
\tau
–dependent
pluri-homogeneous smoothing vector field
X^{\tau}(Z)
in
\Sigma_{p+1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{q}^{-\varrho}
, defined for
\tau\in[0,1]
and some
\varrho\geq 0
,
is
a non–linear map,

\mathcal{F}_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z)=Z+F_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z)Z\,,\qquad\tau\in[0,1]\,,

(2.122)

where
F_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z)
is
a matrix of pluri–homogeneous,
\tau
–dependent, smoothing operators in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, with estimates uniform in
\tau\in[0,1]
,
solving

\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{F}^{\tau}_{\leq N}(Z)=X^{\tau}(\mathcal{F}^{\tau}_{\leq N}(Z))+R^{\tau}_{>N}(Z)Z\,,\quad\mathcal{F}^{0}_{\leq N}(Z)=Z\,,

(2.123)

where
R^{\tau}_{>N}(Z)
is a matrix of
\tau
–dependent, smoothing operators
in
\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, with estimates uniform in
\tau\in[0,1]
.

The following lemma ensures the existence of an approximate flow.

Lemma 2.26
.

(Approximate flow)

Let
p,N\in\mathbb{N}
with
p\leq N
.
Consider a pluri–homogeneous
\tau
–dependent smoothing vector field

X^{\tau}(Z)
in
\Sigma_{p+1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{-\varrho}_{q}
, defined for
\tau\in[0,1]
and some
\varrho\geq 0
.
Then

•

there exists an
approximate flow

\mathcal{F}^{\tau}_{\leq N}
according to Definition
2.25
;

•

denoting by
G_{p}^{\tau}(Z)Z
with

G^{\tau}_{p}(Z)\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
the (p+1)-homogeneous component of

X^{\tau}(Z)
, then the
p
-homogeneous component of the smoothing
operator
F_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z)
in
(
2.122
) is

\mathcal{P}_{p}(F_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z))=\int_{0}^{\tau}G_{p}^{\tau^{\prime}}(Z){\rm d}\tau^{\prime}\,.

(2.124)

Proof.

We write
X^{\tau}(Z):=G^{\tau}(Z)Z
with
G^{\tau}(Z)=\sum_{q=p}^{M}G^{\tau}_{q}(Z)
and

G^{\tau}_{q}(Z)\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
,
and we look for an approximate flow solution of (
2.123
)
of the form

\mathcal{F}_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z)=Z+\sum_{\ell=p}^{N}F^{\tau}_{\ell}(Z,\dots,Z)Z\qquad\text{with}\qquad F_{\ell}^{\tau}(Z)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,.

Since
G^{\tau}(Z)=\sum_{q=p}^{M}G^{\tau}_{q}(Z)
then, using the notation
F_{0}^{\tau}(Z):={\rm Id}
, we expand by multilinearity

X^{\tau}(\mathcal{F}_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z))=\sum_{q=p}^{M}G_{q}^{\tau}(\mathcal{F}_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z),\dots,\mathcal{F}_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z))\mathcal{F}_{\leq N}^{\tau}(Z)=\sum_{\mathtt{a}=p}^{N+(N+1)M}\breve{X}_{\mathtt{a}}^{\tau}(Z)Z

where

\breve{X}_{\mathtt{a}}^{\tau}(Z):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=p,\ldots,M\\
\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q+1}\in\{0,p,\dots,N\}\\
\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q+1}+q=\mathtt{a}\end{subarray}}G_{q}^{\tau}\big(F_{\ell_{1}}^{\tau}(Z)Z,\dots,F_{\ell_{q}}^{\tau}(Z)Z\big)F_{\ell_{q+1}}^{\tau}(Z)\ .

(2.125)

Then we solve (
2.123
) defining recursively for
\mathtt{a}=p,\dots,N
,

F_{\mathtt{a}}^{\tau}(Z):=\int_{0}^{\tau}\breve{X}^{\tau^{\prime}}_{\mathtt{a}}(Z)\,{\rm d}\tau^{\prime},\qquad R_{>N}^{\tau}(Z):=\sum_{\mathtt{a}=N+1}^{N+(N+1)M}X^{\tau}_{\mathtt{a}}(Z)\ .

Using recursively formula (
2.125
) and Proposition
2.15

one verifies that each
\breve{X}_{\mathtt{a}}^{\tau}(Z)
is a
\mathtt{a}
-homogeneous smoothing operator in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{a}}^{-\varrho}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, so is

F_{\mathtt{a}}^{\tau}(Z)
, and

R_{>N}^{\tau}(Z)
is a pluri-homogeneous smoothing operator in
\Sigma_{N+1}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{a}}^{-\varrho}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
Note that for
\mathtt{a}=p
the sum in (
2.125
