Remark 7.16
.

(Integrability of fourth and six order Hamiltonian Birkhoff normal form)

The
\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P}
Hamiltonian monomials (Definition
7.8
)
of degree
4
are integrable. Indeed, a-priori
they are either the integrable ones

|z_{j_{1}}|^{2}|z_{j_{2}}|^{2}
or (i)

|z_{j_{1}}|^{2}z_{j_{2}}\overline{z_{-j_{2}}}
or (ii)
z_{j_{1}}\overline{z_{-j_{1}}}z_{j_{2}}\overline{z_{-j_{2}}}
. The momentum condition
implies in case (i) that
j_{2}=0
, which is not allowed. In case (ii) it yields

j_{1}+j_{2}=0
and so
z_{j_{1}}\overline{z_{-j_{1}}}z_{j_{2}}\overline{z_{-j_{2}}}=z_{j_{1}}\overline{z_{j_{2}}}z_{j_{2}}\overline{z_{j_{1}}}
is integrable. The
\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P}
Hamiltonian monomials of degree
6
may contain the not integrable
monomials

(i)\ |z_{j_{1}}|^{2}z_{j_{2}}\overline{z_{-j_{2}}}z_{j_{3}}\overline{z_{-j_{3}}}\,,\quad(ii)\ |z_{j_{1}}|^{2}|z_{j_{2}}|^{2}z_{j_{3}}\overline{z_{-j_{3}}}\,,\quad(iii)\ z_{j_{1}}\,\overline{z_{-j_{1}}}\,z_{j_{2}}\,\overline{z_{-j_{2}}}\,z_{j_{3}}\,\overline{z_{-j_{3}}}\,.

By momentum conservation, a monomial of the form (
i
) is integrable
(as in case (ii) above) and
a monomial of the form (
ii
) has
j_{3}=0
thus it is not allowed.
The monomials (
iii
)
turn out to be, for
\gamma\neq 0
,
\mathtt{h}=+\infty
,
Birkhoff non-resonant, namely

\displaystyle\big|\Omega_{j_{1}}(\kappa)-\Omega_{-j_{1}}(\kappa)+\Omega_{j_{2}}(\kappa)-\Omega_{-j_{2}}(\kappa)+\Omega_{j_{3}}(\kappa)-\Omega_{-j_{3}}(\kappa)\big|

(7.73)

\displaystyle=\gamma\big|{\rm sign}(j_{1})+{\rm sign}(j_{2})+{\rm sign}(j_{3})\big|\geq\gamma\,.

Therefore they might be eliminated, obtaining an integrable
normal form Hamiltonian at the degree
6
. The same holds in finite depth
exploiting also the momentum restriction
j_{1}+j_{2}+j_{3}=0
and that

\gamma\big(\tanh(\mathtt{h}j_{1})+\tanh(\mathtt{h}j_{2})+\tanh(\mathtt{h}j_{3})\big)\neq 0

by the concavity of
y\mapsto\tanh(\mathtt{h}y)
for
y>0
. Note that for

|j_{1}|,|j_{2}|,|j_{3}|\geq M
large enough we have a uniform lower bound
as in (
7.73
).
In conclusion the fourth and six order Hamiltonian
Birkhoff normal form of the water waves equations (
1.2
) is integrable.

8
Energy estimate and proof of Theorem
1.1

The Hamiltonian equation

\partial_{t}Z=-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)Z+J_{c}\nabla H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}_{\frac{3}{2}}(Z)+J_{c}\nabla H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}_{-\varrho}(Z)\,,

(8.1)

obtained by (
7.21
) neglecting
the symbol and the smoothing operator of homogeneity larger than
N
, preserves the Sobolev norms. Equation (
8.1
) can be also written as the Hamiltonian PDE

\partial_{t}Z=J_{c}\nabla H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}(Z)

(8.2)

where
H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}(Z)
is the super–action preserving Hamiltonian (cfr. Definition
7.8
)

H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}(Z):=H^{(2)}(Z)+H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}_{\frac{3}{2}}(Z)+H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}_{-\varrho}(Z)\,,\quad H^{(2)}(Z):=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\Omega_{j}(\kappa)|z_{j}|^{2}\,.

(8.3)

Actually the following more precise result holds.

Lemma 8.1
.

(Super-actions)

The super–actions
J_{n}(Z)=|z_{n}|^{2}+|z_{-n}|^{2}
, defined in (
7.17
), for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
are prime integrals of the Hamiltonian equation
(
8.1
).
In particular the Sobolev norm

\|Z\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}^{2}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}|j|^{2s}|z_{j}|^{2}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}n^{2s}J_{n}(Z)

is constant along the flow of (
8.1
).

Proof.

By (
8.2
), (
3.29
) and Lemma
7.7
we have

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}J_{n}(Z)={\rm d}_{Z}J_{n}(Z)\left[J_{c}\nabla H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}(Z)\right]=\{J_{n}(Z),H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}(Z)\}=0

since the Hamiltonian
H^{(\mathtt{S}\mathtt{A}\mathtt{P})}(Z)
contains only super action preserving monomials.
∎

By the previous lemma,
in order to derive an energy estimate for the solutions of
(
7.21
), and thus for a solution
U
of (
5.37
),
we have to estimate the non-homogeneous term in (
7.21
).
We need the following lemma.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then there is
s_{0}>0
such that,
for any
s\geq s_{0}
, for all
0<r\leq\overline{r}(s)
small,
if
U
belongs to
B_{s_{0}}^{0}(I;r)\cap C_{*\mathbb{R}}^{0}(I;\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2}))

and solves (
5.37
), then
U
belongs to
C_{*\mathbb{R}}^{K}(I;\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2}))
and
\forall\,0\leq k\leq K

there is a constant
\overline{C}_{s,k}\geq 1
such that

\|\partial_{t}^{k}U(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k}}\leq\overline{C}_{s,k}\|U(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\,,\quad\forall t\in I\,.

(8.4)

In particular the norm

\|U(t)\|_{K,s}
defined in (
2.2
) is equivalent to the norm
\|U(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}
.

Proof.

We argue by induction proving that for any
0\leq k\leq K
, there are
s_{0},\overline{r}_{k}>0
such that if
U\in B_{s_{0}}^{0}(I;\overline{r}_{k})\cap C_{*\mathbb{R}}^{0}(I;\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2}))
solves (
5.37
), then
U\in C^{k}_{*\mathbb{R}}(I;\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
with estimate (
8.4
).
For
k=0
the estimate (
8.4
) is trivial.
Then assume (
8.4
) holds true for
0,\dots,k-1\leq K-1
.
Next we write (
5.37
) as

\partial_{t}U={{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(A(U;x;\xi)\right)}U+R(U)U

where, by Lemma
5.5
, the smoothing operator
R(U)
is in

\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{k-1,0,1}[r,N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
and
the matrix of symbols

A(U;x,\xi):=J_{c}A_{\frac{3}{2}}(U;x)\omega(\xi)+\frac{\gamma}{2}\frac{{\mathtt{G}}(\xi)}{{\rm i}\xi}\mathds{1}+J_{c}\big[A_{1}(U;x,\xi)+A_{\frac{1}{2}}(U;x,\xi)+A^{(2)}_{0}(U;x,\xi)]

belongs to
\Sigma\Gamma_{k-1,0,0}^{\frac{3}{2}}[r,N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
and so,
by the fourth bullet after Definition
2.16
,

{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(A(U;x,\xi)\right)}
belongs to
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{k-1,0,0}[r,N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
Let
s_{0}:=s_{0}(k)>0
given in Definitions
2.5
,
2.16
. By the inductive estimate (
8.4
) up to
k-1
, we have that

U\in B_{s_{0}}^{k-1}(I,\overline{C}_{s_{0},k}^{\prime}r)
with

\overline{C}_{s_{0},k}^{\prime}:=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\overline{C}_{s_{0},j}
.
Then, for any
s\geq s_{0}
, there is
\overline{r}_{k}:=\overline{r}(s,k)>0
such that if
0<r<\overline{r}_{k}

the operator
{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(A(U;x,\xi)\right)}
fulfills estimate below (
2.72
) (with
K^{\prime}=0
,
m=\frac{3}{2}
), and
R(U)
estimate below
(
2.34
) (with
K^{\prime}=0
,
m=0
) so that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}U\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{t}^{k-1}({{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(A(U;x,\xi)\right)}U)\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}(k-1)-\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\partial_{t}^{k-1}(R(U)U)\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}(k-1)-\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s,k}\|U\|_{k-1,s}=C_{s,k}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\|\partial_{t}^{j}U\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}j}}\leq\overline{C}_{s,k}\|U\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}

(8.5)

by the inductive hypothesis (
8.4
) for
j=0,\dots,k-1
and setting
\overline{C}_{s,k}:=C_{s,k}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\overline{C}_{s,j}
. This proves
(
8.4
) at step
k
.
We finally fix
\overline{r}(s):=\min(\overline{r}_{1},\dots,\overline{r}_{K})
proving the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1

We deal only with the case
N\in\mathbb{N}
, since the cubic energy estimate
(
8.15
)
in case
N=0
follows directly from Proposition
6.1
(see also Remark
6.2
), yielding the local time of existence of order
\varepsilon^{-1}
.

So from now on we consider
N\in\mathbb{N}
. For any value of the gravity
g>0
, depth
\mathtt{h}\in(0,+\infty]
and vorticity
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
,
let
{\mathcal{K}}\subset(0,+\infty)
be the zero measure set
defined in Theorem
B.1
.
Assume that the surface tension coefficient
\kappa
belongs to the complementary set
(0,+\infty)\setminus{\mathcal{K}}
.
Let
\underline{\varrho}>0
be the constant given by Proposition
7.12
.

•

Choice of the parameters:

From now on we fix in Proposition
7.12
the parameters

\varrho:=\underline{\varrho}\,,\quad K:=\underline{K}^{\prime}(\varrho)\in\mathbb{N}\,,

(8.6)

where
\underline{K}^{\prime}(\varrho)
is defined in Proposition
6.1
.
Thus Proposition
7.12
provides

\underline{s}_{0}>0
and for any

s\geq\underline{s}_{0}\,\quad\text{we fix}\quad 0<r<\min(\underline{r}_{0}(s),\overline{r}(s))\,,

(8.7)

where
\underline{r}_{0}(s)>0
is given by Proposition
7.12
and
\overline{r}(s)>0
is given by Lemma
8.2
. Let

U(t)
be a solution of system (
5.37
) in
B_{\underline{s}_{0}}^{K}(I;r)\cap C_{*\mathbb{R}}^{K}(I;\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{2}))
.

In order to prove Theorem
1.1
we have to provide energy estimates of the
non-homogenous “vector field” in (
7.21
):

X_{\geq N+2}(U,Z):=\begin{pmatrix}X^{+}_{\geq N+2}(U,Z)\\
\overline{X^{+}_{\geq N+2}(U,Z)}\end{pmatrix}:={{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(-{\rm i}(\mathtt{m}_{\frac{3}{2}})_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}Z+R_{>N}(U;t)U\,.

(8.8)

The following lemma holds since the imaginary part of the
x
–independent symbol
(\mathtt{m}_{\frac{3}{2}})_{>N}(U;t,\xi)
has order zero and because, with the choice of
\varrho=\underline{\varrho}
in (
8.6
), the remainder
R_{>N}(U;t)
in (
8.8
) belongs to
\mathcal{R}^{0}_{K,\underline{K}^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Lemma 8.3
.

(Energy estimate)

The non-homogenous vector field
X_{\geq N+2}(U,Z)
defined in (
8.8
), where
(\mathtt{m}_{\frac{3}{2}})_{>N}
and
R_{>N}(U;t)
are defined in Proposition
7.12
with parameters given in (
8.6
), (
8.7
),
satisfies, for any
t\in I
, the energy estimate

\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\mathbb{T}}|D|^{s}\,X_{\geq N+2}^{+}(U,Z)\cdot\overline{|D|^{s}z}\,{\rm d}x\leq C_{s,K}\|Z\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}^{N+3}\,.

(8.9)

Proof.

Since
(\mathtt{m}_{\frac{3}{2}})_{>N}(U;t,\xi)
is
x
–independent
and has imaginary part
\mathrm{Im}\,(\mathtt{m}_{\frac{3}{2}})_{>N}
in
\Gamma_{K,\underline{K}^{\prime},N+1}^{0}[r]

(cfr. Proposition
7.12
)
then
{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\big(-{\rm i}(\mathtt{m}_{\frac{3}{2}})_{>N}\big)

commutes with the derivatives

|D|^{s}
and, by (
2.72
) (with
k=0
,
m=0
,
s=0
and
K=\underline{K}^{\prime}
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\mathbb{T}}|D|^{s}{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(-{\rm i}(\mathtt{m}_{\frac{3}{2}})_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}z\cdot|D|^{s}\overline{z}\,{\rm d}x

(8.10)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\mathbb{T}}{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(\mathrm{Im}\,(\mathtt{m}_{\frac{3}{2}})_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}|D|^{s}z\cdot|D|^{s}\overline{z}\,{\rm d}x\lesssim\|U\|_{\underline{K}^{\prime},s_{0}}^{N+1}\|Z\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma \ref{equiv-tempo}},\eqref{equivalenzan}}}{{\lesssim_{s,K}}}\|Z\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}^{N+3}\,.

We consider now the contribution coming from the
smoothing operator
R_{>N}(U;t)
in
\mathcal{R}^{0}_{K,\underline{K}^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
Using (
2.34
) (with
k=0
,
m=0
), Lemma
8.2
and (
7.23
) we get

\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\mathbb{T}}|D|^{s}\big(R_{>N}(U;t)U\big)^{+}\cdot|D|^{s}\overline{z}\,{\rm d}x\lesssim_{s}\|U\|_{\underline{K}^{\prime},s_{0}}^{N+1}\|U\|_{\underline{K}^{\prime},s}\|Z\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\lesssim_{s,K}\|Z\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}^{N+3}\,.

(8.11)

The estimate (
8.9
) follows by (
8.10
) and (
8.11
).
∎

We now prove the following key bootstrap result.
By time reversibility we may, without loss of generality, consider only positive times
t>0
.

Proposition 8.4
.

(Bootstrap)

For any
s\geq\underline{s}_{0}
there exist
c_{0}:=c_{0}(s,K)\in(0,1)
, such that for any solution
U(t)\in C^{K}_{*\mathbb{R}}(I;\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}^{2}))
of (
5.37
) fulfilling, for some
0<\varepsilon_{1}<\min(\underline{r}_{0}(s),\overline{r}(s))
,

{\|U(0)\|}_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq c_{0}\varepsilon_{1}\,,\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\|\partial_{t}^{k}U(t)\|}_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k}}\leq\varepsilon_{1}\,,\qquad T\leq c_{0}\varepsilon_{1}^{-N-1}\,,

(8.12)

then we have the improved bound

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\|\partial_{t}^{k}U(t)\|}_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k}}\leq\dfrac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2}\,.

(8.13)

Proof.

By (
8.12
) we have that
U\in B^{K}_{s}(I;\varepsilon_{1})
with
I:=[-T,T]
.
By Proposition
7.12
(applied with
r\leadsto\varepsilon_{1}<\underline{r}_{0}(s)
), the variable

Z(t):=\mathcal{F}_{\mathtt{nf}}(Z_{0}(t)),\quad Z_{0}(t):=\big({\rm Id}+R({\bf B}(U(t);t)U(t))\big){\bf B}(U(t);t)U(t)\,,

where
{\bf B}(U;t)
is defined in Proposition
6.1
and the smoothing operator
R(\cdot)
is defined in (
7.3
),
solves (
7.21
) and has a Sobolev norm equivalent to that of
U(t)
, see (
7.23
).
Lemmata
8.1
and
8.3
(using also
\varepsilon_{1}<\min(\underline{r}_{0}(s),\overline{r}(s))
) imply that the solution
Z(t)
of system (
7.21
) satisfies the energy estimate

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\|Z(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}^{2}\leq C_{s,K}\|Z\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}^{N+3}

(8.14)

and therefore
for all
0\leq t\leq T
, by (
7.23
),

\displaystyle{\|U(t)\|}_{\dot{H}^{s}}^{2}\leq C_{s,K}{\|Z(t)\|}^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{stimaEW}}}{{\leq}}C_{s,K}\left({\|Z(0)\|}^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}}+\int_{0}^{t}{\|Z(\tau)\|}^{N+3}_{\dot{H}^{s}}\,{\rm d}\tau\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equivalenzan}}}{{\leq}}C_{s,K}^{\prime}{\|U(0)\|}^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}}+C_{s,K}^{\prime}\int_{0}^{t}\|U(\tau)\|^{N+3}_{\dot{H}^{s}}\,{\rm d}\tau\,.

(8.15)

Then, by the a priori assumption (
8.12
)
we deduce that, for all
0\leq t\leq T\leq c_{0}\varepsilon_{1}^{-(N+1)}
,

{\|U(t)\|}^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq C_{s,K}^{\prime}c^{2}_{0}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}+C_{s,K}^{\prime}\,{T}\,\varepsilon_{1}^{N+3}\leq\varepsilon_{1}^{2}(C_{s,K}^{\prime}c_{0}^{2}+C_{s,K}^{\prime}c_{0})\,.

(8.16)

The desired conclusion (
8.13
)
on the norms
C^{k}_{t}H^{s-\frac{3}{2}k}_{x}

follows by Lemma
8.2
, (
8.16
),
choosing
c_{0}
small enough depending on
s
and
K
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1
concluded.

Step 1: Local existence.

By the local existence theory, there exist

r_{{\rm loc}},s_{{\rm loc}}>0
such that for any
s\geq s_{{\rm loc}}
there are
T_{\rm loc}>0,C_{\rm loc}\geq 1
such that, any initial datum
(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})\in H_{0}^{s+\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{T})\times{\dot{H}}^{s-\frac{1}{4}}
with

\|\eta_{0}\|_{H_{0}^{s+\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\psi_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{1}{4}}}\leq r_{{\rm loc}}

there exists a unique classical solution
(\eta(t),\psi(t))
in

C^{0}\bigl([-T_{\rm loc},T_{\rm loc}],H_{0}^{s+\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{T})\times{\dot{H}}^{s-\frac{1}{4}}(\mathbb{T})\bigr)
of (
1.2
) satisfying the initial condition
\eta(0)=\eta_{0},\psi(0)=\psi_{0}
and

\sup_{t\in[-T_{\rm loc},T_{\rm loc}]}\big(\|\eta(t)\|_{H_{0}^{s+\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\psi(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{1}{4}}}\big)\leq C_{\rm loc}\big(\|\eta_{0}\|_{H^{s+\frac{1}{4}}_{0}}+\|\psi_{0}\|_{{\dot{H}}^{s-\frac{1}{4}}}\big)\,.

(8.17)

Remark 8.5
.

The local existence result can be derived arguing as in
[
43
,
22
]
(which have the very same paradifferential functional setting developed in this paper).
One could also extend the proof of
[
2
]
to the case of constant vorticity.

Step 2: Complex variables.

System (
5.37
) and the water waves equations (
1.2
) are equivalent under the linear change of variables

U=\begin{pmatrix}u\\
\overline{u}\end{pmatrix}=\mathcal{M}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{W}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{pmatrix},\quad u=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}M(D)^{-1}\eta+\frac{{\rm i}}{\sqrt{2}}M(D)\zeta\,,\quad\zeta:=\psi-\frac{\gamma}{2}\partial_{x}^{-1}\eta\,,

(8.18)

defined in (
5.2
), (
5.6
).
In view of (
8.18
) we have the equivalence of the norms: for some
\mathtt{C}:=\mathtt{C}(\mathtt{h},\gamma,\kappa)\geq 1
, any

t

\mathtt{C}^{-1}\|U(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq{\|\eta(t)\|}_{H_{0}^{s+\frac{1}{4}}}+{\|\psi(t)\|}_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{1}{4}}}\leq\mathtt{C}\|U(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\,.

(8.19)

Step 3: Bootstrap argument.

Consider the local solution of (
1.2
) with initial datum
(\eta_{0},\psi_{0})
satisfying

{\|\eta_{0}\|}_{H_{0}^{s+\frac{1}{4}}}+{\|\psi_{0}\|}_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{1}{4}}}\leq\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}

(8.20)

where

\varepsilon_{0}:=\min\Big(\frac{\underline{r}_{0}(s)}{\breve{C}_{s,K}},\frac{\overline{r}(s)}{\breve{C}_{s,K}},r_{{\rm loc}}\Big)\,,\quad\breve{C}_{s,K}:=\max\Big(\frac{\mathtt{C}}{c_{0}(s,K)},K\overline{C}_{s,K}\mathtt{C}C_{{\rm loc}}\Big)\,.

(8.21)

By (
8.19
), (
8.20
), (
8.21
),
Lemma
8.2
(with
r\leadsto\mathtt{C}C_{{\rm loc}}\varepsilon_{0}<\overline{r}(s)
) and
(
8.17
) we deduce that

\|U(0)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq\mathtt{C}\varepsilon,\quad\|\partial_{t}^{k}U(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k}}\leq\overline{C}_{s,K}\mathtt{C}C_{{\rm loc}}\varepsilon\,,\quad\forall 0<t<T_{{\rm loc}}\,,\ \forall k=0,\dots,K\,.

(8.22)

By (
8.22
) and
(
8.21
), the solution
U(t)
of (
5.37
) satisfies, for any
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
, the smallness condition

{\|U(0)\|}_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq c_{0}\varepsilon_{1}\,,\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\rm loc}]}\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\|\partial_{t}^{k}U(t)\|}_{\dot{H}^{s-\frac{3}{2}k}}\leq\varepsilon_{1}\quad\text{with}\quad\varepsilon_{1}:=\breve{C}_{s,K}\varepsilon\,,

which is (
8.12
) with
T=T_{{\rm loc}}
.
Proposition
8.4
and a standard bootstrap argument imply that
the maximal time
T_{\rm max}\geq T_{{\rm loc}}

of existence of the solution
U(t)
is larger than
T_{\varepsilon}:=c_{0}\varepsilon_{1}^{-N-1}
and

\|U(t)\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
for any
0\leq t<T_{\varepsilon}
.
By (
8.18
) this proves that the solution

(\eta,\psi)(t)=\mathcal{W}{\mathcal{M}}U(t)
of the water waves equations (
1.2
) satisfies
(
1.7
) and (
1.8
)
with
c:=c_{0}\breve{C}_{s,K}^{-N-1}
and
C:=\mathtt{C}\breve{C}_{s,K}
.

Appendix A
Conjugation lemmata

We collect in this Appendix important results used along the paper about how paradifferential equations are conjugated under the flow of an unbounded Fourier multiplier
(Lemma
A.4
)
and an approximate flow generated by a smoothing vector field (Lemma
A.5
).

The following result about the approximate inverse is a consequence of Lemma
2.24
.

Lemma A.1
.

Assume (
3.35
) with
U
in
B^{K}_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}(I;r)
,

K,K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
K^{\prime}\leq K
,
r>0
.
Let
p,N\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\leq N
,
m\geq 0
, and consider

W:=\Phi(Z)=Z+S(Z;t)Z

(A.1)

where

S(Z;t)\in\begin{cases}\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\qquad\qquad\text{if}\quad{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)={\rm Id}\,,\\
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,0,p}[\breve{r},N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,,\,\forall\breve{r}>0\quad\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

(A.2)

Then we may write

Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U\quad\text{with}\quad\Psi_{\leq N}(W):=W+\breve{S}_{\leq N}(W)W\,,

(A.3)

where

•

\breve{S}_{\leq N}(W)
is a matrix of pluri-homogeneous spectrally localized maps
in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m(N-p+1)}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
;

•

M_{>N}(U;t)
is a matrix of non-homogeneous operators in

\mathcal{M}^{m(N-p+2)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.24
there exists
an approximate inverse up to homogeneity
N
of
the pluri-homogeneous nonlinear map (obtained by (
A.1
))

\Phi_{\leq N}(Z):=Z+\mathcal{P}_{\leq N}[S(Z;t)]Z

having the form

\Psi_{\leq N}(W)=W+\breve{S}_{\leq N}(W)W\quad\text{with}\quad\breve{S}_{\leq N}(W)=\sum_{q=p}^{N}\breve{S}_{q}(W)\,,\quad\breve{S}_{q}(W)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m(N-p+1)}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,.

Applying
\Psi_{\leq N}
to (
A.1
), writing
\Phi(Z)=\Phi_{\leq N}(Z)+S_{>N}(Z;t)Z

with

S_{>N}(Z;t):=S(Z;t)-\mathcal{P}_{\leq N}[S(Z;t)]
,
we get

\displaystyle\Psi_{\leq N}(W)=\Psi_{\leq N}\big(\Phi_{\leq N}(Z)+S_{>N}(Z;t)Z\big)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{\leq N}\big(\Phi_{\leq N}(Z)\big)+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{1}{\rm d}_{W}\Psi_{\leq N}\left(\Phi_{\leq N}(Z)+\tau S_{>N}(Z;t)Z\right)[S_{>N}(Z;t)Z]{\rm d}\tau}_{=:S^{\prime\prime}_{>N}(Z;t)Z}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{invmappe}}}{{=}}Z+S^{\prime}_{>N}(Z)Z+S^{\prime\prime}_{>N}(Z;t)Z

(A.4)

where
S^{\prime}_{>N}(Z)\in\Sigma_{N+1}{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}}_{q}^{m(N-p+2)}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

by Lemma
2.24
-(
i
)
and, according to (
A.2
), by Proposition
2.19
-(
v
)

S^{\prime\prime}_{>N}(Z;t)\in\begin{cases}\mathcal{S}^{m(N-p+2)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\qquad\qquad\ \ \text{if}\quad{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)={\rm Id}\,,\\
\mathcal{S}^{m(N-p+2)}_{K,0,N+1}[\breve{r}]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})\,,\,\forall\breve{r}>0\quad\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

(A.5)

Finally we substitute
Z={\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U

where
{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)
is in
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},0}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

(cfr. (
3.35
)) in the non–homogeneous term
S^{\prime}_{>N}(Z)Z
and
S^{\prime\prime}_{>N}(Z;t)Z

in (
A.4
)-(
A.5
) and using
(iii)
and
(i)
Proposition
2.15
we
deduce (
A.3
) and that

M_{>N}(U;t)\in\mathcal{M}^{m(N-p+2)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
∎

We provide a lemma concerning how paradifferential and smoothing operators change by substituting in the ‘internal” variables a close to the identity map.

Lemma A.2
.

Assume
W={\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U
with
{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)\in\mathcal{M}_{K,K^{\prime},0}^{0}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
,
U\in B_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}^{K}(I;r)
for some
r,s_{0}>0
and
0\leq K^{\prime}\leq K
.
Let
p,N\in\mathbb{N}
with
p\leq N
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
and
consider a nonlinear map

Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U\quad\text{where}\quad\Psi_{\leq N}(W)=W+M_{\leq N}(W)W

(A.6)

with

\bullet

M_{\leq N}(W)
is a matrix of pluri-homogeneous
m
-operators in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{q}^{m}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
;

\bullet

M_{>N}(U;t)
is a matrix of non homogeneous
m
-operators in
\mathcal{M}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}^{m}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Then

(i)

(
Symbols
)
if
a_{\leq N}(Z;\xi)
is a pluri-homogeneous real-valued symbol, independent of
x
, in
\Sigma_{2}^{N}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{q}^{m^{\prime}}
,
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\displaystyle\rm{{{\displaystyle Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(a_{\leq N}(Z;\xi)\right)}_{|Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U}

(A.7)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle={{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(a^{+}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)+a^{+}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}+R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)+R_{>N}^{+}(U;t)\,,

where

\bullet

a^{+}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)
is a pluri-homogeneous real-valued symbol independent of
x
in
\Sigma_{2}^{N}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{m^{\prime}}_{q}
such that

\mathcal{P}_{\leq p+1}(a^{+}_{\leq N}(W;\xi))=\mathcal{P}_{\leq p+1}(a_{\leq N}(W;\xi))\,;

(A.8)

\bullet

a^{+}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)
is a non-homogeneous real valued symbol independent of
x
in
\Gamma^{m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
;

\bullet

R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)
is a pluri-homogeneous smoothing operator in
\Sigma_{p+2}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\varrho\geq 0
;

\bullet

R_{>N}^{+}(U;t)
is a non-homogeneous smoothing operator in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
for any
\varrho\geq 0
.

(ii)

(
Smoothing operators
) If
R_{\leq N}(Z)
is a pluri-homogeneous smoothing operator in
\Sigma_{1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}
, for some
\varrho\geq 0
, then

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle R_{\leq N}(Z)Z)_{|Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U}=R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)W+R_{>N}^{+}(U;t)U\,,

(A.9)

\displaystyle R_{\leq N}(Z)_{|Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U}=R^{\prime}_{\leq N}(W)+R_{>N}^{\prime}(U;t)

where

\bullet

R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)
and
R^{\prime}_{\leq N}(W)
are pluri-homogeneous smoothing operators in
\Sigma_{1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho+(N+1)\max(m,0)}_{q}
such that

\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}(R^{+}_{\leq N}(W))=\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}(R^{\prime}_{\leq N}(W))=\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}(R_{\leq N}(W))\,;

(A.10)

\bullet

R_{>N}^{+}(U;t)
and
R_{>N}^{\prime}(U;t)
are non-homogeneous smoothing operators in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+(N+1)\max(m,0)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.

If
\varrho\leq(N+1)\max(m,0)
we regard
R^{+}_{\leq N}
and
R_{>N}^{+}
as operators in
\Sigma_{1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}^{-\varrho+(N+1)\max(m,0)}_{q}
and respectively
\mathcal{M}^{-\varrho+(N+1)\max(m,0)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.

Remark A.3
.

The previous lemma is stated for
x
independent symbols (since it is used in this case)
but it holds also for a general symbol.

Proof.

Proof of
(i)
:
We expand by multilinearity the operator in (
A.7
).
We denote the homogeneous components of
M_{\leq N}(W)
in (
A.6
) as

M_{\ell}(W):=\mathcal{P}_{\ell}(M_{\leq N}(W))
for
\ell=p,\dots,N
and
M_{0}(W):={\rm Id}
.
Note that

(M_{\ell}(W)W)_{|W={\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U}=M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell}(U;t)U

where

M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell}(U;t):=M_{\ell}({\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U){\bf M}_{0}(U;t)

belongs to
\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},\ell}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for
\ell\in\{0,p,\dots,N\}

thanks to
(i)
and
(ii)
of
Proposition
2.15
.
Then by multilinearity decompose the operator in (
A.7
) as

\displaystyle{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(a_{\leq N}(Z;\xi)\right)}_{|Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathtt{a}=2}^{N}\sum_{q=2}^{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q}\in\{0,p,\dots,N\}\\
\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}+q=\mathtt{a}\end{subarray}}{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(a_{q}(Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q};\xi)\right)}_{|Z_{1}=M_{\ell_{1}}(W)W,\dots,Z_{q}=M_{\ell_{q}}(W)W}

(A.11)

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=2}^{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q}\in\{0,p,\dots N\}\\
\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}+q\geq N+1\end{subarray}}{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(a_{q}(Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q};\xi)\right)}_{|Z_{1}=M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{1}}(U;t)U,\dots,Z_{q}=M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{q}}(U;t)U}

(A.12)

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=2}^{N}\,\sum_{n=0}^{q-1}\,\!\!\sum_{\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{n}\in\{0,p,\dots N\}}\binom{q}{n}\,{{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(a_{q}(Z_{1},\dots,Z_{q};\xi)\right)}_{\big|\begin{smallmatrix}&Z_{1}=M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{1}}(U;t)U,\dots,Z_{n}=M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{n}}(U;t)U\\
&Z_{n+1}=\dots=Z_{q}=M_{>N}(U;t)U\end{smallmatrix}}.

(A.13)

By
(iv)
of Proposition
2.15
we have that

\eqref{omosomma}={{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(a_{\leq N}^{+}(W)\right)}+R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)\quad\text{with}\quad R_{\leq N}^{+}(W)\in\Sigma_{2}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\,,\ \forall\varrho\geq 0\,,

(A.14)

where
a_{\leq N}^{+}(W)=\sum_{\mathtt{a}=2}^{N}a^{+}_{\mathtt{a}}(W;\xi)
and

\quad a^{+}_{\mathtt{a}}(W;\xi):=\sum_{q=2}^{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q}\in\{0,p,\dots,N\}\\
\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}+q=\mathtt{a}\end{subarray}}a_{q}(M_{\ell_{1}}(W)W,\dots,M_{\ell_{q}}(W)W;\xi)

(A.15)

belongs to
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{m^{\prime}}_{\mathtt{a}}
.
For
\mathtt{a}\leq p+1
the sum in (
A.11
) and
(
A.15
) reduces to the indices
q=\mathtt{a}
,
\ell_{1}=\dots=\ell_{\mathtt{a}}=0
. As a consequence
a^{+}_{\mathtt{a}}(W;\xi)=a_{\mathtt{a}}(W;\xi)
for
\mathtt{a}=2,\dots,p+1
, proving (
A.8
).
For the same reason the remainder
R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)
in (
A.14
) actually belongs to
\Sigma_{p+2}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}
.

Now we consider the non-homogeneous terms which arise from lines (
A.12
) and (
A.13
). Thanks to
(iv)
of Proposition
2.15
we have that

\eqref{nonomosomma}+\eqref{nonomosomma2}={{{\rm Op}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left(a^{+}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}+R^{+}_{>N}(U;t)

where
R_{>N}^{+}(U;t)
is a
smoothing operator in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
for any
\varrho\geq 0
, and

a^{+}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)

is a non–homogeneous symbol in
\Gamma^{m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
which is real valued and
x
-independent as well as
a_{\leq N}
.

Proof of
(ii)
:
Proceeding in similarly to
(i)
we expand the left hand side of (
A.9
) as

\displaystyle(R_{\leq N}(Z)Z)_{|Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathtt{a}=1}^{N}\sum_{q=1}^{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q+1}\in\{0,p,\dots,N\}\\
\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q+1}+q=\mathtt{a}\end{subarray}}R_{q}(M_{\ell_{1}}(W)W,\dots,M_{\ell_{q}}(W)W)M_{\ell_{q+1}}(W)W

(A.16)

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=1}^{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q}\in\{0,p,\dots N\}\\
\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q}+q\geq N+1\end{subarray}}R_{q}(M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{1}}(U;t)U,\dots,M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{q}}(U;t)U)M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{q+1}}(U;t)U

(A.17)

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=1}^{N}\,\sum_{n=0}^{q-1}\,\sum_{\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{n+1}\in\{0,p,\dots N\}}\binom{q}{n}\,R_{q}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{q})M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{n+1}}(U;t)U_{\big|\begin{smallmatrix}&Z_{1}=M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{1}}(U;t)U,\dots,Z_{n}=M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{n}}(U;t)U\\
&Z_{n+1}=\dots=Z_{q}=M_{>N}(U;t)U\end{smallmatrix}}

(A.18)

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=1}^{N}\,\sum_{n=0}^{q-1}\,\sum_{\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{n}\in\{0,p,\dots N\}}\binom{q}{n}\,R_{q}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{q})M_{>N}(U;t)U_{\big|\begin{smallmatrix}&Z_{1}=M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{1}}(U;t)U,\dots,Z_{n}=M^{({\bf M}_{0})}_{\ell_{n}}(U;t)U\\
&Z_{n+1}=\dots=Z_{q}=M_{>N}(U;t)U\end{smallmatrix}}.

(A.19)

Thanks to
(ii)
of Proposition
2.15
(with
m\leadsto-\varrho
,
m_{\ell}\leadsto m
), the term in (
A.16
) can be written as
R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)W
where
R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)
is a pluri–homogeneous smoothing operator in
\Sigma_{1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho+(N+1)\max(m,0)}_{q}
, moreover
R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)=\sum_{\mathtt{a}=1}^{N}R_{\mathtt{a}}^{+}(W)
with

R_{\mathtt{a}}^{+}(W):=\sum_{q=1}^{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{q+1}\in\{0,p,\dots,N\}\\
\ell_{1}+\dots+\ell_{q+1}+q=\mathtt{a}\end{subarray}}R_{q}(M_{\ell_{1}}(W)W,\dots,M_{\ell_{q}}(W)W)M_{\ell_{q+1}}(W)\,.

(A.20)

For
\mathtt{a}\leq p
the sum in (
A.20
) reduces to the indices
q=\mathtt{a}
,
\ell_{1}=\dots=\ell_{\mathtt{a}+1}=0
. As a consequence
R^{+}_{\mathtt{a}}(W)=R_{\mathtt{a}}(W)
for
\mathtt{a}=1,\dots,p
, proving (
A.10
).
Applying again Proposition
2.15

(ii)
we get that the terms in (
A.17
)–(
A.19
) can be written as
R_{>N}^{+}(U;t)U
where
R_{>N}^{+}(U;t)
is a non–homogenous smoothing operator in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+(N+1)\max(m,0)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
. This concludes the proof of the first identity in (
A.9
). The second one follows with the same analysis, without the need of substitute the last variable.
∎

Conjugation lemmata.

The following
conjugation Lemmata
A.4
and
A.5

are used in the nonlinear Hamiltonian Birkhoff normal form reduction performed in Section
7
.

The following hypothesis shall be assumed in both Lemmata
A.4
and
A.5
:

Assumption (A)
:
Assume
Z:={\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U
where
{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},0}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
,
U\in B^{K}_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}(I;r)
for some
r,s_{0}>0
and
0\leq K^{\prime}\leq K
.
Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and assume that
Z
solves the system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Z=-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)Z+{{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}a_{\leq N}(Z;\xi)+{\rm i}a_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}Z+R_{\leq N}(Z)Z+R_{>N}(U;t)U

(A.21)

where
{\bf\Omega}(D)
is the diagonal matrix of Fourier multiplier operators defined in (
5.10
) and

•

a_{\leq N}(Z;\xi)
is a real valued pluri-homogenous
symbol, independent of
x
, in
\Sigma_{2}^{N}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{q}
;

•

a_{>N}(U;t,\xi)
is a non-homogenous symbol, independent of
x
, in
\Gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
with imaginary part

{\rm Im}\,a_{>N}(U;t,\xi)
in
\Gamma^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
;

•

R_{\leq N}(Z)
is a real-to-real matrix of pluri-homogeneous smoothing operators in
\Sigma_{1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
;

•

R_{>N}(U;t)
is a real-to-real matrix of
non-homogeneous smoothing operators
in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

We also write system (
A.21
) in the form

\partial_{t}Z=-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)Z+M_{\leq N}(Z)Z+M_{>N}(U;t)U

(A.22)

where

M_{\leq N}(Z)
are
\frac{3}{2}
-operators in
\Sigma_{1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{q}^{\frac{3}{2}}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
and
M_{>N}(U;t)
is in
\mathcal{M}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
by the fourth remark below Definition
2.16
.

Lemma A.4

(
Conjugation under the flow of a Fourier multiplier).

Assume
(A)
at page
A
.
Let
g_{p}(Z;\xi)
be a
p
–homogeneous real symbol independent of
x
in
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{p}
,
p\geq 2
,
that we expand as

g_{p}(Z;\xi)=\sum_{(\vec{\jmath}_{p},\vec{\sigma}_{p})\in{\mathfrak{T}}_{p}}G_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p}}(\xi)z_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p}}\,,\quad\overline{G_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}^{-\vec{\sigma}_{p}}}(\xi)=G_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p}}(\xi)\in\mathbb{C}

(A.23)

and denote by
\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z):=\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}^{1}(Z)
the time
1
-flow defined in (
3.56
) generated by
{{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}g_{p}(Z;\xi)\right)}
.
If
Z(t)
solves system (
A.21
), then the variable

W:=\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)Z

(A.24)

solves the system

\partial_{t}W=-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)W+{{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}a_{\leq N}^{+}(W;\xi)+{\rm i}a_{>N}^{+}(U;t,\xi)\right)}W+R_{\leq N}^{+}(W)W+R^{+}_{>N}(U;t)U

(A.25)

where

•

a^{+}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)
is a real valued pluri-homogenous symbol, independent of
x
, in
\Sigma_{2}^{N}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{q}
, with components

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\leq p-1}[a^{+}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)]=\mathcal{P}_{\leq p-1}[a_{\leq N}(W;\xi)]\,,

(A.26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{p}\left[a^{+}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)\right]=\mathcal{P}_{p}\left[a_{\leq N}(W;\xi)\right]+g^{+}_{p}(W;\xi)

where
g^{+}_{p}(W;\xi)\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{p}
is the real,
x
-independent symbol

g^{+}_{p}(W;\xi):=\sum_{(\vec{\jmath}_{p},\vec{\sigma}_{p})\in{\mathfrak{T}}_{p}}-{\rm i}\big(\vec{\sigma}_{p}\cdot\Omega_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}(\kappa)\big)G_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p}}(\xi)w_{\vec{\jmath}_{p}}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p}};

(A.27)

•

a^{+}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)
is a non-homogeneous symbol, independent of
x
, in
\Gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
with imaginary part
{\rm Im}\,a_{>N}^{+}(U;t,\xi)

belonging to
\Gamma^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
;

•

R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)
is a real-to-real matrix of pluri–homogeneous smoothing operators in
\Sigma_{1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho+c(N,p)}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for some
c(N,p)>0
(depending only on
N,p
) and fulfilling

\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}[R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)]=\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}[R_{\leq N}(W)]\,;

(A.28)

•

R^{+}_{>N}(U;t)
is a real-to-real matrix of non–homogeneous smoothing operators in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+c(N,p)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

Proof.

Since
Z(t)
solves (
A.21
) then differentiating (
A.24
) we get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}W

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)\big[-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)+{{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}a_{\leq N}(Z;\xi)+{\rm i}a_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}\big]\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)^{-1}W

(A.29)

\displaystyle\ \ +\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)\,R_{\leq N}(Z)Z+\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)R_{>N}(U;t)U

(A.30)

\displaystyle\ \ +(\partial_{t}\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z))\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)^{-1}W.

(A.31)

We now compute (
A.29
)–(
A.31
) separately. As
\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)
is the time
1
-flow of a Fourier multiplier it commutes with every Fourier multiplier, and (
A.29
) is equal to

\eqref{paraspazio}=-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)W+{{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}a_{\leq N}(Z;\xi)+{\rm i}a_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}W\,.

(A.32)

Now we write the symbol
a_{\leq N}(Z;\xi)
in terms of
W
.
By Lemma
3.17

(iii)
we have that

\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)-{\rm Id}
is a matrix of spectrally localized maps in
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{\frac{3}{2}(N+1)}_{K,0,p}[\breve{r},N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\breve{r}>0
.
By Assumption
(A)
we have
Z={\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U

with
{\bf M}_{0}(U;t)\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},0}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
Lemma
A.1
(with
S(Z;t)=\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)-{\rm Id}
) provides
an approximate inverse of
W=\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)Z
of the form

Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U,\quad\Psi_{\leq N}(W):=W+\breve{S}_{\leq N}(W)W

(A.33)

where
\breve{S}_{\leq N}(W)
is a matrix of spectrally localized maps in

\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{q}^{\frac{3}{2}(N+1)(N-p+1)}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
and
M_{>N}(U;t)
is in

\mathcal{M}^{\frac{3}{2}(N+1)(N-p+2)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
The map (
A.33
) has the form (
A.6
), so by Lemma
A.2

(i)

(with
m^{\prime}=3/2
) we obtain

\displaystyle{{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}a_{\leq N}(Z;\xi)\right)}_{|Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U}

\displaystyle={{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}a^{\prime}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)+{\rm i}a^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}+R^{\prime}_{\leq N}(W)+R_{>N}^{\prime}(U;t)\ ,

(A.34)

where

\bullet

a^{\prime}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)
is a real valued pluri-homogenous symbol independent of
x
in

\Sigma_{2}^{N}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{q}
with

\mathcal{P}_{\leq p+1}(a^{\prime}_{\leq N}(W;\xi))=\mathcal{P}_{\leq p+1}(a_{\leq N}(W;\xi))\,;

(A.35)

\bullet

a^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)
is a non-homogenous real valued
symbol independent of
x
in
\Gamma_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}^{\frac{3}{2}}[r]
;

\bullet

R^{\prime}_{\leq N}(W)

is a real-to-real matrix of pluri-homogeneous smoothing operators in
\Sigma_{p+2}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})

for any
\varrho\geq 0
;

\bullet

R^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t)
is a real-to-real matrix of non-homogeneous smoothing operators in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\varrho\geq 0
.

We now consider the terms in (
A.30
).
Since
\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)-{\rm Id}
belongs to
\Sigma\mathcal{S}^{\frac{3}{2}(N+1)}_{K,0,p}[r,N]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
by Lemma
3.17
,
Proposition
2.19
-
(i)
implies that

\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)R_{\leq N}(Z)Z=R^{\prime}_{\leq N}(Z)Z+R^{\prime}_{>N}(Z;t)Z

(A.36)

where
R^{\prime}_{\leq N}(Z)
is in
\Sigma_{1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho+\frac{3}{2}(N+1)}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
with

\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}[R_{\leq N}^{\prime}(Z)]=\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}[R_{\leq N}(Z)]

(A.37)

and

R^{\prime}_{>N}(Z;t)
is
in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+\frac{3}{2}(N+1)}_{K,0,N+1}[\breve{r}]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for any
\breve{r}>0
.
We now substitute
Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U
(cfr. (
A.33
)) in the homogeneous components of (
A.36
) and
Z={\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U
in the non-homogeneous ones of (
A.36
) and (
A.30
).
So using Lemma
A.2
(with
m\leadsto\tfrac{3}{2}(N+1)(N-p+2)
,
\varrho\leadsto\varrho-\tfrac{3}{2}(N+1)
),

(i)
and
(iii)
of Proposition
2.15
,
(i)
and
(iv)
of Proposition
2.19
we get

(
A.30
)

\displaystyle=(R^{\prime}_{\leq N}(Z)Z)_{|Z=\Psi_{\leq N}(W)+M_{>N}(U;t)U}+R^{\prime}_{>N}({\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U;t){\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}({\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U)R_{>N}(U;t)U=R^{\prime\prime}_{\leq N}(W)W+R^{\prime\prime}_{>N}(U;t)U\,,

(A.38)

where

\bullet

R_{\leq N}^{\prime\prime}(W)
is a pluri-homogeneous smoothing operator in

\Sigma_{1}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{q}^{-\varrho+c(N,p)}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
with

and

\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}[R_{\leq N}^{\prime\prime}(W)]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{invariatosmo}}}{{=}}\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}[R^{\prime}_{\leq N}(W)]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{lem.conj.R}}}{{=}}\mathcal{P}_{\leq p}[R_{\leq N}(W)]\ ;

(A.39)

\bullet

R_{>N}^{\prime\prime}(U;t)
is a non–homogeneous smoothing operator in

\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+c(N,p)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

We finally consider the last term (
A.31
). Using that
\mathcal{G}_{g_{p}}(Z)
commutes with every Fourier multiplier we get

\eqref{paratempo}={{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}\partial_{t}g_{p}(Z;\xi)\right)}W\,.

(A.40)

So, using (
A.22
), (
A.23
) and the identity
(-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)Z)_{j}^{\sigma}=-{\rm i}\sigma\Omega_{j}(\kappa)z_{j}^{\sigma}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{p}(Z;\xi)=\ \sum_{a=1}^{p}g_{p}(\overbrace{Z,\dots,Z}^{a-\text{times}},-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)Z,Z,\dots,Z;\xi)+p\,g_{p}(M_{\leq N}(Z)Z,Z,\dots,Z;\xi)

(A.41)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+p\,g_{p}(M_{>N}(U;t)U,Z,\dots,Z;\xi)_{|Z={\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U}

\displaystyle=g^{+}_{p}(Z;\xi)+p\,g_{p}(\mathcal{P}_{\leq N-p}(M_{\leq N})(Z)Z,Z,\dots,Z;\xi)

\displaystyle\quad+p\,g_{p}(\mathcal{P}_{>N-p}(M_{\leq N})(Z)Z,Z,\dots,Z;\xi)_{|Z={\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U}+p\,g_{p}(M_{>N}(U;t)U,Z,\dots,Z;\xi)_{|Z={\bf M}_{0}(U;t)U}

\displaystyle=g^{+}_{p}(Z;\xi)+g^{\prime}_{\geq p+1}(Z;\xi)+g^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)

where
g^{+}_{p}
is the real valued symbol in
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{p}
in (
A.27
), the real valued pluri-homogeneous symbol
g^{\prime}_{\geq p+1}
is in
\Sigma_{p+1}^{N}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{q}
thanks to
(iv)
of Proposition
2.15
and
the real valued
non-homogeneous symbol

is in

using also
(ii)
of Proposition
2.15
.
Then by (
A.40
), (
A.41
) and using the second part of
(iv)
of Proposition
2.15

we obtain

\eqref{paratempo}={{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}g^{+}_{p}(Z;\xi)+{\rm i}g^{\prime}_{\geq p+1}(Z;\xi)+{\rm i}g^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}W+R^{\prime}_{\geq p+1}(Z)W+R^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t)U

(A.42)

where

R^{\prime}_{\geq p+1}
is a matrix of pluri-homogeneous smoothing operators in
\Sigma_{p+1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
and

R^{\prime}_{>N}(U;t)
belongs to
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.
Then we substitute the variable
Z
in (
A.31
) using (
A.33
) and Lemma
A.2
, to obtain

\eqref{paratempo}={{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}g^{+}_{p}(W;\xi)+{\rm i}g^{+}_{\geq p+1}(W;\xi)+{\rm i}g^{+}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}W+R^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\leq N}(W)W+R^{\prime\prime\prime}_{>N}(U;t)U

(A.43)

where

\bullet

g^{+}_{p}(W;\xi)
is the homogeneous symbol in (
A.27
);

\bullet

g^{+}_{\geq(p+1)}(W;\xi)
is a pluri-homogeneous real valued symbol in
\Sigma_{p+1}^{N}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{q}
;

\bullet

g^{+}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)
is a non-homogeneous real valued symbol in
\Gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
;

\bullet

R^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\leq N}(W)
is a matrix of pluri-homogeneous operators in
\Sigma_{p+1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho+c(N,p)}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
;

\bullet

R^{\prime\prime\prime}_{>N}(U;t)
is a matrix of smoothing operators in

\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho+c(N,p)}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

In conclusion, combining (
A.32
), (
A.34
), (
A.39
) and (
A.43
) we deduce (
A.25
)
with
a^{+}_{\leq N}=a^{\prime}_{\leq N}+g_{p}^{+}+g_{\geq p+1}^{+}
and
a^{+}_{>N}:=a_{>N}+a^{\prime}_{>N}+g^{+}_{>N}
which has imaginary part equal to the one of
a_{>N}
belonging to
\Gamma^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
.
Moreover (
A.26
) follows by (
A.35
) and (
A.28
) by (
A.38
).
∎

The following lemma describes how a system is conjugated under
a smoothing perturbation of the identity.

Lemma A.5

(
Conjugation under a smoothing perturbation of the identity).

Assume
(A)
at page
A
.
Let
F_{\leq N}(Z)
be a real-to-real matrix of pluri-homogeneous smoothing operators
in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{q}^{-\varrho^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
for some
\varrho^{\prime}\geq 0
.
If
Z(t)
solves (
A.21
)
then the variable

W:=\mathcal{F}_{\leq N}(Z):=Z+F_{\leq N}(Z)Z

(A.44)

solves

\partial_{t}W=-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)W+{{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}a^{+}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)+{\rm i}a^{+}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}W+R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)W+R^{+}_{>N}(U;t)U

(A.45)

where

•

a^{+}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)
is a real valued pluri-homogenous symbol, independent of
x
, in
\Sigma_{2}^{N}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{q}
, with components

\mathcal{P}_{\leq p+1}[a^{+}_{\leq N}(W;\xi)]=\mathcal{P}_{\leq p+1}[a_{\leq N}(W;\xi)]\,;

(A.46)

•

a^{+}_{>N}(U;t,\xi)
is a non-homogeneous symbol, independent of
x
, in
\Gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
with imaginary part

{\rm Im}\,a_{>N}^{+}(U;t,\xi)
belonging to
\Gamma^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]
;

•

R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)
is a real-to-real matrix of
pluri–homogeneous smoothing operators in

\Sigma_{1}^{N}{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho_{*}}_{q}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
,

\varrho_{*}:=\min{(\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}-\frac{3}{2})}
(
\varrho\geq 0
is the smoothing order in Assumption
(A)
at page
A
), with components

\mathcal{P}_{\leq p-1}[R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)]=\mathcal{P}_{\leq p-1}[R_{\leq N}(W)]\ ,

(A.47)

and, denoting
F_{p}(W):=\mathcal{P}_{p}(F_{\leq N}(W))
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho^{\prime}}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
, one has

\mathcal{P}_{p}[R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)]=\mathcal{P}_{p}[R_{\leq N}(W)]+{\rm d}_{W}\big(F_{p}(W)W\big)[-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)]+{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)F_{p}(W);

(A.48)

•

R^{+}_{>N}(U;t)
is a real-to-real matrix of non–homogeneous smoothing operators in
\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho_{*}}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[r]\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
.

In addition, if
\mathcal{F}_{\leq N}(Z)
in (
A.44
) is the approximate time

-flow (given by Lemma

) of a vector field

G_{p}(Z)Z
, where

has Fourier expansion

\big(G_{p}(Z)Z\big)^{\sigma}_{k}=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(\vec{\jmath}_{p+1},k,\vec{\sigma}_{p+1},-\sigma)\in{\mathfrak{T}}_{p+2}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!G_{\vec{\jmath}_{p+1},k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p+1},\sigma}z_{\vec{\jmath}_{p+1}}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p+1}}\,,

(A.49)

then (

) reduces to

\mathcal{P}_{p}[R^{+}_{\leq N}(W)]=\mathcal{P}_{p}[R_{\leq N}(W)]+G_{p}^{+}(W)

(A.50)

where
G_{p}^{+}(W)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho^{\prime}+\frac{3}{2}}_{p}\otimes\mathcal{M}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
is the smoothing operator with Fourier expansion

\displaystyle(G_{p}^{+}(W)W)^{\sigma}_{k}=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(\vec{\jmath}_{p+1},k,\vec{\sigma}_{p+1},-\sigma)\in{\mathfrak{T}}_{p+2}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!-{\rm i}\big(\vec{\sigma}_{p+1}\cdot\Omega_{\vec{\jmath}_{p+1}}(\kappa)-\sigma\Omega_{k}(\kappa)\big)G_{\vec{\jmath}_{p+1},k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p+1},\sigma}\,w_{\vec{\jmath}_{p+1}}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p+1}}\,.

(A.51)

Proof.

Since

(A.52)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+R_{\leq N}(Z)Z+{\rm d}_{Z}(F_{\leq N}(Z)Z)[-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)Z]

(A.53)

(A.54)

\displaystyle+R_{>N}(U;t)U+{\rm d}_{Z}(F_{\leq N}(Z)Z){{{\rm Op}_{\mathtt{vec}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{B}W}}}}\left({\rm i}a_{>N}(U;t,\xi)\right)}Z+{\rm d}_{Z}(F_{\leq N}(Z)Z)[R_{>N}(U;t)U]\,.

(A.55)

\displaystyle+{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)F_{p}(Z)Z+R_{\geq p+1}(Z)Z+R_{>N}(U;t)U

with

) and

(-{\rm i}{\bf\Omega}(D)Z)_{j}^{\sigma}=-{\rm i}\sigma\Omega_{j}(\kappa)z_{j}^{\sigma}
.
Note that the smoothing properties of

can also be directly verified by the characterization of Lemma
