.
∎

Appendix B
Non–resonance conditions

The goal of this section is to prove that the linear
frequencies
\vec{\Omega}(\kappa)
, defined in (
7.16
)
and (
5.12
),
satisfy, for any value of the
gravity
g
, vorticity
\gamma
and depth
\mathtt{h}
,
the following non-resonance properties, except a zero measure set of
surface tension coefficients
\kappa
.

Theorem B.1
.

(Non-resonance)
Let
M\in\mathbb{N}
. For any
g>0
,
\mathtt{h}\in(0,+\infty]
and
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a zero measure set
{\mathcal{K}}\subset(0,+\infty)

such that, for any compact interval
[\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}]\subset(0,+\infty)

there is
\tau>0
and, for any
\kappa\in[\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}]\setminus{\mathcal{K}}

the following holds: there is a positive constant
\nu>0
such that
for any multi-index

(\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}

of length
|\alpha+\beta|\leq M
,
which is not super action preserving
(cfr. Definition
7.3
), it results

|\vec{\Omega}(\kappa)\cdot(\alpha-\beta)|>\frac{\nu}{\Big(\max\limits_{j\in\textup{supp}(\alpha\cup\beta)}|j|\Big)^{\tau}}

(B.1)

where
\textup{supp}(\alpha\cup\beta):=\{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\colon\alpha_{j}+\beta_{j}\not=0\}
.

Theorem
B.1
extends
Proposition 8.1 in
[
16
]
, which is valid only in the irrotational case

\gamma=0
and in finite depth.
Theorem
B.1
follows by the next result
where we fix a
compact interval of surface tension coefficients putting
\mathcal{K}:=\cap_{\nu>0}\mathcal{K}_{\nu}
.

Proposition B.2
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{N}
and
fix a compact interval
\mathcal{I}:=[\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}]
with
0<\kappa_{1}<\kappa_{2}
.
Then there exist
\nu_{M},\tau,\delta>0
such that for any
\nu\in(0,\nu_{M})
,
there is a set
\mathcal{K}_{\nu}\subset\mathcal{I}
of measure
O(\nu^{\delta})

such that for any
\kappa\in\mathcal{I}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{\nu}
the following holds:
for any multi-index
(\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}

of length
|\alpha+\beta|\leq M
,
which is not super action preserving (cfr. Definition
7.3
),
one has

|\vec{\Omega}(\kappa)\cdot(\alpha-\beta)|>\frac{\nu}{\Big(\max\limits_{j\in\textup{supp}(\alpha\cup\beta)}|j|\Big)^{\tau}}\,.

(B.2)

The proof makes use of
Delort-Szeftel Theorem 5.1 of
[
38
]
about measure estimates
for sublevels of subanalytic functions, whose statement is the following.

Theorem B.3

(Delort-Szeftel)
.

Let
X
be a closed ball
\overline{B(0,r_{0})}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}

and
Y
a compact interval of
\mathbb{R}
.
Let
f\colon X\times Y\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuous subanalytic function,
\rho:X\to\mathbb{R}
a real analytic function,
\rho\not\equiv 0
. Assume

(H1)

f
is real analytic on
\{x\in X\colon\rho(x)\neq 0\}\times Y
;

(H2)

for all
x_{0}\in X
with
\rho(x_{0})\neq 0
, the equation
f(x_{0},y)=0
has only finitely many solutions
y\in Y
.

Then there are
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha_{0}>0
,
\delta>0
,
C>0
, such that for any
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
, any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
N\geq N_{0}
, any
x
with
\rho(x)\neq 0
,

{\rm meas}\big\{y\in Y\colon\ \ \ |f(x,y)|\leq\alpha|\rho(x)|^{N}\big\}\leq C\alpha^{\delta}\,|\rho(x)|^{N\delta}\,.

We shall first prove Proposition
B.2
for deep water,
in
Section
B.1
, and then, for any
finite depth, in Section
B.2
.

B.1
Deep-water case

In the deep water case
\mathtt{h}=+\infty
,
by (
5.12
) and (
5.5
),
the linear frequencies are

\Omega_{j}(\kappa)=\omega_{j}(\kappa)+\frac{\gamma}{2}\,{\rm sign}(j)\,,\quad\omega_{j}(\kappa)=\sqrt{(\kappa j^{2}+g)|j|+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}}\,.

(B.3)

In this case Proposition
B.2
is a consequence of the following result.

Proposition B.4
.

Let
\mathcal{I}=[\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}]
and
consider two integers
\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then there exist
\alpha_{0},\tau,\delta>0
(depending on
\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M}
)
such that for any
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0})
, there is a
set
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\subset\mathcal{I}
of measure
O(\alpha^{\delta})
,
such that for any
\kappa\in\mathcal{I}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}
the following holds:
for any

1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}
,
any

\vec{c}:=(c_{0},c_{1},\ldots,c_{\mathtt{A}})\in\mathbb{Z}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{A}}
, with

|\vec{c}\,|_{\infty}:=\max_{a=0,\ldots,\mathtt{A}}|c_{a}|\leq\mathtt{M}
, one has

\Big|{\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}c_{a}\,\omega_{n_{a}}(\kappa)+\frac{\gamma}{2}c_{0}}\Big|>\frac{\alpha}{(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a})^{\tau}}.

(B.4)

Before proving Proposition
B.4
we deduce as a corollary Proposition
B.2

when
\mathtt{h}=+\infty
.

Proof of Proposition
B.2
.

For any multi-index
(\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
with length
|\alpha+\beta|\leq M
,
using that
\omega_{j}(\kappa)
in (
B.3
) is even in
j
,
we get

\displaystyle\vec{\Omega}(\kappa)\cdot(\alpha-\beta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\omega_{j}(\kappa)(\alpha_{j}-\beta_{j})+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\gamma}{2}{\rm sign}(j)(\alpha_{j}-\beta_{j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n>0}\omega_{n}(\kappa)(\alpha_{n}+\alpha_{-n}-\beta_{n}-\beta_{-n})+\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{n>0}(\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{-n}-\beta_{n}+\beta_{-n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)}\omega_{n}(\kappa)(\alpha_{n}+\alpha_{-n}-\beta_{n}-\beta_{-n})+\frac{\gamma}{2}c_{0}

(B.5)

where

\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)
is the set
defined in (
7.10
) and

c_{0}:=\sum_{n>0}\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{-n}-\beta_{n}+\beta_{-n}\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Since
(\alpha,\beta)
is not super action preserving (cfr. Definition
7.3
)
then
\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)
is not empty.
By (
7.11
) the cardinality

\mathtt{A}:=|\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)|
satisfies
1\leq\mathtt{A}\leq M
.
Denoting by
1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}

the distinct elements of
\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)
, and the integer numbers

c_{a}:=\alpha_{n_{a}}+\alpha_{-n_{a}}-\beta_{n_{a}}-\beta_{-n_{a}}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\,,\quad\forall a=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}\,,

we deduce by (
B.5
)
that

\vec{\Omega}(\kappa)\cdot(\alpha-\beta)=\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}\omega_{n_{a}}(\kappa)c_{a}+\frac{\gamma}{2}c_{0}\,.

By the definition of
\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)
, each integer

c_{a}\neq 0
,
a=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}
, and
|c_{a}|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq M
.
Similarly
|c_{0}|\leq M
.
Applying Proposition
B.4
with
\mathtt{M}=M
we deduce (
B.2
) with
\nu:=\frac{\alpha}{M^{\tau}}
.
∎

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
B.4
.

Proof of Proposition
B.4
.

For any
\vec{n}:=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{A}}
with

1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}
, we denote

x_{0}(\vec{n}):=\frac{1}{\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}}\,,\qquad x_{a}(\vec{n}):=x_{0}(\vec{n})\sqrt{n_{a}}\,,\quad\forall\,a=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}\,.

(B.6)

Clearly

0<\tfrac{1}{\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}}\leq x_{a}(\vec{n})\leq 1\ ,\qquad\forall\,a=0,\ldots,\mathtt{A}\,.

(B.7)

If (
B.4
) holds, then multiplying it by
x_{0}(\vec{n})^{3}
, one gets that the inequalities

\Big|{\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}c_{a}\,\sqrt{\kappa x_{a}^{6}+gx_{a}^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}x_{0}^{6}}+\frac{\gamma}{2}c_{0}x_{0}^{3}}\Big|\geq\alpha\,x_{0}^{\tau+3}

(B.8)

hold at any
x_{a}=x_{a}(\vec{n})
,
a=0,\ldots,\mathtt{A}
, defined in (
B.6
).
This suggests to define the function

\lambda(y,x_{0},\kappa):=\sqrt{\kappa y^{6}+gy^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}x_{0}^{6}}\,,

(B.9)

and, for
\vec{c}=(c_{0},c_{1},\ldots,c_{\mathtt{A}})\in\mathbb{Z}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{A}}
,

f_{\vec{c}}\,\colon[-1,1]^{\mathtt{A}+1}\times\mathcal{I}\to\mathbb{R}\,,\quad f_{\vec{c}}\,(x,\kappa):=\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}c_{a}\,\lambda(x_{a},x_{0},\kappa)+\frac{\gamma}{2}c_{0}x_{0}^{3}

(B.10)

where
x:=(x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{\mathtt{A}})
.

We estimate the sublevels of
\kappa\mapsto f_{\vec{c}}(x,\kappa)
using Theorem
B.3
. Let us verify its assumptions. The set
X:=[-1,1]^{\mathtt{A}+1}
is a closed ball in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathtt{A}+1}
.
The function
f_{\vec{c}}:X\times\mathcal{I}\to\mathbb{R}
is continuous and subanalytic.
Then we define the non-zero real analytic function

\rho(x):=\prod_{a=0}^{\mathtt{A}}x_{a}\,\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq\mathtt{A}}(x_{a}^{2}-x_{b}^{2})\,.

(B.11)

We observe that
\rho(x)
evaluated at

x(\vec{n}):=(x_{0}(\vec{n}),\ldots,x_{\mathtt{A}}(\vec{n}))
,
defined in (
B.6
), satisfies

\Big(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}\Big)^{-\tau_{1}}\leq|{\rho(x(\vec{n}))}|\lesssim_{\mathtt{A}}\Big(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}\Big)^{-1}

(B.12)

with
\tau_{1}:=\mathtt{A}+1+2\binom{\mathtt{A}}{2}
,
as follows by (
B.7
), (
B.11
)
and the assumption that the
n_{a}
’s are all distinct, thus

|n_{a}-n_{b}|\geq 1
, for any
a\neq b
.

We show now that the assumptions (H1) and (H2) of Theorem
B.3
hold true.

Verification of (H1).

If
\rho(x)\neq 0
then, by (
B.11
),

x_{a}\neq 0\,,\ \forall\,0\leq a\leq\mathtt{A}\,,\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad x_{a}^{2}\neq x_{b}^{2}\,,\ \forall\,1\leq a<b\leq\mathtt{A}\,.

(B.13)

In particular on the set
\{x\in X\colon\rho(x)\neq 0\}\times Y
the function

\lambda(x_{a},x_{0},\kappa)
in (
B.9
) in real analytic and thus the function

f_{\vec{c}}(x,\kappa)
in (
B.10
) is real analytic.

Verification of (H2).
The fact that, for any
x\in X

such that
\rho(x)\neq 0
, the
analytic
function
\kappa\mapsto f_{\vec{c}}(x,\kappa)

possesses only a finite number of zeros on the interval

\mathcal{I}
, is
a consequence of the next lemma.

Lemma B.5
.

For any
x\in X

such that
\rho(x)\neq 0
, the
function
\kappa\mapsto f_{\vec{c}}(x,\kappa)
is not identically zero in
\mathcal{I}
.

Proof.

We argue by contradiction, assuming that there
exists
x=(x_{a})_{0\leq a\leq\mathtt{A}}\in X
with
\rho(x)\neq 0

such that
f_{\vec{c}}(x,\kappa)=0
for any
\kappa
in the interval
\mathcal{I}
.
Then
the function
\kappa\mapsto f_{\vec{c}}(x,\kappa)

is identically zero also on the larger domain
of analyticity
(-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}x_{0}^{6},+\infty)
.
Note that
x_{0}^{2}>0
because
\rho(x)\neq 0
, cfr. (
B.13
).
In particular, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
, all the derivatives

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}f_{\vec{c}}(x,\kappa)\equiv 0
are zero
in the interval
(-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}x_{0}^{6},+\infty)
.

Now we compute such derivatives at
\kappa=0
by differentiating (
B.10
).
The derivatives of the function
\lambda(y,x_{0},\kappa)
defined in (
B.9
) are
given by, for suitable constants
C_{l}\neq 0
,

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}\lambda(y,x_{0},\kappa)=C_{l}y^{6l}\,\lambda(y,x_{0},\kappa)^{1-2l}\,,\quad\forall l\in\mathbb{N}\,.

Thus we obtain

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}\lambda(y,x_{0},\kappa)|_{\kappa=0}=C_{l}\,\mu(y,x_{0})^{l}\,\lambda(y,x_{0},0)\qquad\text{where}\qquad\mu(y,x_{0}):=\frac{y^{6}}{gy^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}x_{0}^{6}}\,,

(B.14)

and, recalling (
B.10
),

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}f_{\vec{c}}(x,\kappa)|_{\kappa=0}=C_{l}\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}c_{a}\mu(x_{a},x_{0})^{l}\,\lambda(x_{a},x_{0},0)\,,\quad\forall l\in\mathbb{N}\,.

As a consequence, the conditions

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}f_{\vec{c}}\,(x,\kappa)|_{\kappa=0}=0
for any
l=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}
, imply that

A(x)\vec{{\mathfrak{c}}}=0

(B.15)

where
A(x)
is the
\mathtt{A}\times\mathtt{A}
-matrix

A(x):=\begin{pmatrix}\mu(x_{1},x_{0})\lambda(x_{1},x_{0},0)&\cdots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})\lambda(x_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0},0)\\
\mu(x_{1},x_{0})^{2}\lambda(x_{1},x_{0},0)&\cdots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})^{2}\lambda(x_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0},0)\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mu(x_{1},x_{0})^{\mathtt{A}}\lambda(x_{1},x_{0},0)&\ldots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})^{\mathtt{A}}\lambda(x_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0},0)\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}:=\begin{bmatrix}c_{1}\\
\vdots\\
c_{\mathtt{A}}\end{bmatrix}\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{A}}\,.

Since the vector
\vec{{\mathfrak{c}}}\neq 0
, we deduce by (
B.15
) that
the matrix
A(x)
has zero determinant.
On the other hand, by the multi-linearity of the determinant,

\displaystyle\det A(x)

\displaystyle=\prod_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}\mu(x_{a},x_{0})\lambda(x_{a},x_{0},0)\det\begin{pmatrix}1&\cdots&1\\
\mu(x_{1},x_{0})&\cdots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mu(x_{1},x_{0})^{\mathtt{A}-1}&\ldots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})^{\mathtt{A}-1}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\prod_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}\mu(x_{a},x_{0})\lambda(x_{a},x_{0},0)\,\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq\mathtt{A}}(\mu(x_{a},x_{0})-\mu(x_{b},x_{0}))

(B.16)

by a Vandermonde determinant.
The condition
\rho(x)\neq 0

implies, by (
B.13
), (
B.14
) and (
B.9
), that

\prod_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}\mu(x_{a},x_{0})\lambda(x_{a},x_{0},0)\neq 0\,,

and,
in view of (
B.16
), the determinant

\det A(x)=0
if and only if
\mu(x_{a},x_{0})=\mu(x_{b},x_{0})
for some
1\leq a<b\leq\mathtt{A}
.
Since the function
y\to\mu(y,x_{0})
in (
B.14
) is even and strictly monotone on the two intervals
(0,+\infty)
and
(-\infty,0)
, it follows that

\mu(x_{a},x_{0})=\mu(x_{b},x_{0})\quad\Rightarrow\quad|x_{a}|=|x_{b}|\,.

This contradicts
\rho(x)\neq 0
, see (
B.13
). The lemma is proved.
∎

We have verified assumptions (H1) and (H2) of Theorem
B.3
.
We thus conclude that
there are
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha_{0},\delta,C>0
, such that for any
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
, any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
N\geq N_{0}
, any
x\in X
with
\rho(x)\neq 0
,

{\rm meas}\big\{\kappa\in\mathcal{I}\colon\ \ \ |f_{\vec{c}}(x,\kappa)|\leq\alpha|\rho(x)|^{N}\big\}\leq C\alpha^{\delta}\,|\rho(x)|^{N\delta}\,.

(B.17)

For any
\vec{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{A}}
with
1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}
, we consider the set

\mathcal{B}_{\vec{c},\vec{n}}(\alpha,N):=\big\{\kappa\in\mathcal{I}\colon\ \ \ |f_{\vec{c}}(x(\vec{n}),\kappa)|\leq\alpha|\rho(x(\vec{n}))|^{N}\big\}

(B.18)

where
x(\vec{n})=(x_{a}(\vec{n}))_{a=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}}\in X
is defined in (
B.6
).
By (
B.12
) we get

\rho(x(\vec{n}))\neq 0
. Then (
B.17
) yields

{\rm meas}\ \mathcal{B}_{\vec{c},\vec{n}}(\alpha,N)\leq C\alpha^{\delta}|\rho(x(\vec{n}))|^{N\delta}.

(B.19)

Consider the set

\mathcal{K}(\alpha,N):=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{A}},\,1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}\\
\vec{c}\in\mathbb{Z}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{A}},\,|\vec{c}\,|_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{M}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{B}_{\vec{c},\vec{n}}(\alpha,N)\subset\mathcal{I}\,.

(B.20)

By (
B.19
) and (
B.12
) it results

{\rm meas}\ \mathcal{K}(\alpha,N)\leq C(\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M})\alpha^{\delta}\sum_{n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}}\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a})^{N\delta}}\leq C^{\prime}(\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M})\alpha^{\delta}

(B.21)

for some finite constant
C^{\prime}(\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M})<+\infty
, provided

N\delta>\mathtt{A}
. We fix

\underline{N}:=[\mathtt{A}\delta^{-1}]+1\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathcal{K}}_{\alpha}:={\mathcal{K}}(\alpha,\underline{N})

whose measure satisfies

|{\mathcal{K}}_{\alpha}|\lesssim_{\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M}}\alpha^{\delta}
by (
B.21
).
For any
\kappa\in\mathcal{I}\setminus{\mathcal{K}}_{\alpha}
, for any

\vec{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}})
with
1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}
,
for any
\vec{c}\in\mathbb{Z}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{A}}
with

|\vec{c}\,|_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{M}
,
one has, by
(
B.20
) and (
B.18
),

|{f_{\vec{c}}(x(\vec{n}),\kappa)}|>\alpha|\rho(x(\vec{n}))|^{\underline{N}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{rho.estg}}}{{\geq}}\frac{\alpha}{\left(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}\right)^{\tau_{1}\underline{N}}}\,.

(B.22)

Recalling the definition of
f_{\vec{c}}
in (
B.10
), (
B.9
) and
x_{0}(\vec{n})
in (
B.6
),
the lower bound (
B.22
)
implies (
B.4
) with
\tau:=\tau_{1}\underline{N}-3
, cfr. (
B.8
).
∎

B.2
Finite depth case

We consider now the finite depth case
0<\mathtt{h}<+\infty
where the frequencies are, by (
5.12
) and (
5.5
),

\Omega_{j}(\kappa)=\omega_{j}(\kappa)+\frac{\gamma}{2}\,\tanh(\mathtt{h}j)\,,\quad\omega_{j}(\kappa)=\sqrt{j\tanh(\mathtt{h}j)\Big(\kappa j^{2}+g+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\frac{\tanh(\mathtt{h}j)}{j}\Big)}\,.

(B.23)

In this case Proposition
B.2
is a consequence of the following result.

Proposition B.6
.

Let
\mathcal{I}=[\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}]
and
consider
\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathtt{B}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
Then there exist
\alpha_{0},\tau,\delta>0
(depending on
\mathtt{A},\mathtt{B},\mathtt{M}
) such that for any
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0})
, there is a set
\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}\subset\mathcal{I}

of measure
O(\alpha^{\delta})
,
such that for any
\kappa\in\mathcal{I}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}
the following holds:
for any
1\leq n_{1}\leq\ldots\leq n_{\mathtt{A}}

any
\vec{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathtt{B}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{B}}
, any

\vec{c}:=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{\mathtt{A}})\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{A}}
with

|\vec{c}\,|_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{M}
and

\vec{d}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{\mathtt{B}})\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{B}}
with
|\vec{d}\,|_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{M}
,
one has

\Big|\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}c_{a}\,\omega_{n_{a}}(\kappa)+\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}d_{b}\tanh(\mathtt{h}m_{b})\Big|>\frac{\alpha}{(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}+\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}m_{b})^{\tau}}\,.

(B.24)

If
\mathtt{B}=0
, by definition, the sums in (
B.24
) in the index

b
are empty and the vectors
\vec{m},\vec{d}
are not present.

Before proving Proposition
B.6
we deduce
Proposition
B.2

in the case of finite depth
\mathtt{h}
.

Proof of Proposition
B.2
.

Let
(\alpha,\beta)\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}
be a
multi-index with length
|\alpha+\beta|\leq M
,
which is not super action preserving (cfr. Definition
7.3
).
Denote
by
1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}
the elements
of
\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)

defined in (
7.10
), and let
\mathtt{A}:=|\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)|\geq 1
.
We also consider the set

\mathfrak{M}(\alpha,\beta):=\{n\in\mathbb{N}\colon\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{-n}-\beta_{n}+\beta_{-n}\not=0\}
, which could be empty.
We denote by
\mathtt{B}:=|\mathfrak{M}(\alpha,\beta)|
its cardinality, which could be zero,
and
1\leq m_{1}<\dots<m_{\mathtt{B}}
its distinct elements, if any.
By (
7.11
) it results
1\leq\mathtt{A}\leq M
and
0\leq\mathtt{B}\leq M
(arguing as for (
7.11
)).
By (
B.23
) and
since
\omega_{j}(\kappa)
is even in
j
,
we get

\displaystyle\vec{\Omega}(\kappa)\cdot(\alpha-\beta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\omega_{j}(\kappa)(\alpha_{j}-\beta_{j})+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\gamma}{2}\tanh(\mathtt{h}j)(\alpha_{j}-\beta_{j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n>0}\omega_{n}(\kappa)(\alpha_{n}+\alpha_{-n}-\beta_{n}-\beta_{-n})+\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{n>0}\tanh(\mathtt{h}n)(\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{-n}-\beta_{n}+\beta_{-n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)}\omega_{n}(\kappa)(\alpha_{n}+\alpha_{-n}-\beta_{n}-\beta_{-n})+\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{n\in\mathfrak{M}(\alpha,\beta)}\tanh(\mathtt{h}n)(\alpha_{n}-\alpha_{-n}-\beta_{n}+\beta_{-n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}\omega_{n_{a}}(\kappa)c_{a}+\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}d_{b}\tanh(\mathtt{h}m_{b})

(B.25)

having defined

c_{a}:=\alpha_{n_{a}}+\alpha_{-n_{a}}-\beta_{n_{a}}-\beta_{-n_{a}}\,,\qquad d_{b}:=\alpha_{m_{b}}-\alpha_{-m_{b}}-\beta_{m_{b}}+\beta_{-m_{b}}\,.

By the definition of
\mathfrak{N}(\alpha,\beta)
,
each
c_{a}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}

and
|c_{a}|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq M
for any
a=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}
.
If
\mathfrak{M}(\alpha,\beta)
is empty then
\mathtt{B}=0
, and the second sum in
(
B.25
) in the index
b
is not present. On the other hand, if

\mathtt{B}\geq 1
,
by the definition of

\mathfrak{M}(\alpha,\beta)
, each
d_{b}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
and

|d_{b}|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq M
for any
b=1,\dots,\mathtt{B}
.
Applying in both cases
Proposition
B.6
with
\mathtt{M}=M
we deduce (
B.2
)
with
\nu:=\frac{\alpha}{(2M)^{\tau}}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
B.6
.

We write the proof in the case
\mathtt{B}\geq 1
.
In the case
\mathtt{B}=0
the same argument works.
For any
\vec{n}:=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{A}}

with
1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}

and
\vec{m}:=(m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathtt{B}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{B}}
we define

\displaystyle x_{0}(\vec{n},\vec{m}):=\frac{1}{\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}+\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}m_{b}}\,,\qquad x_{a}(\vec{n},\vec{m}):=x_{0}(\vec{n},\vec{m})\sqrt{n_{a}}\,,\ \forall\,a=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}\,

(B.26)

\displaystyle t_{a}(\vec{n}):=\sqrt{\tanh(\mathtt{h}n_{a})}\,,\ \ \forall\,a=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}\,,\quad t_{\mathtt{A}+b}(\vec{m}):=\sqrt{\tanh(\mathtt{h}m_{b})}\,,\ \ \forall\,b=1,\dots,\mathtt{B}\,.

Clearly

\displaystyle 0<\tfrac{1}{\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}+\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}m_{b}}\leq x_{a}(\vec{n},\vec{m})\leq 1\,,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\tanh(\mathtt{h})}\leq t_{a}(\vec{n})\leq 1\,,\qquad\forall a=0,\ldots,\mathtt{A}\,,

(B.27)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\tanh(\mathtt{h})}\leq t_{\mathtt{A}+b}(\vec{m})\leq 1\,,\quad\forall\,b=1,\ldots,\mathtt{B}\,.

If (
B.24
) holds, then multiplying it by
x_{0}(\vec{n},\vec{m})^{3}
,
one gets, recalling (
B.23
), that the inequalities

\Big|{\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}c_{a}t_{a}\,\sqrt{\kappa x_{a}^{6}+gx_{a}^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}t_{a}^{2}x_{0}^{6}}+\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}d_{b}t_{\mathtt{A}+b}^{2}x_{0}^{3}}\Big|\geq\alpha\,x_{0}^{\tau+3}

(B.28)

hold at any
x_{0}=x_{0}(\vec{n},\vec{m})
,
x_{a}=x_{a}(\vec{n},\vec{m})
,
t_{a}=t_{a}(\vec{n})
,
a=1,\dots,\mathtt{A}
and
t_{\mathtt{A}+b}=t_{\mathtt{A}+b}(\vec{m})
,
b=1,\dots,\mathtt{B}
, defined in (
B.26
).
This suggests to define the function

\lambda(y,s,x_{0},\kappa):=\sqrt{\kappa y^{6}+gy^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}s^{2}x_{0}^{6}}\,,

(B.29)

and, for
\vec{c}:=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{\mathtt{A}})\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{A}}

and
\vec{d}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{\mathtt{B}})\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{B}}
,

f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,\colon[-1,1]^{2\mathtt{A}+\mathtt{B}+1}\times\mathcal{I}\to\mathbb{R}\,,\quad f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa):=\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}c_{a}t_{a}\,\lambda(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0},\kappa)+\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}d_{b}t_{\mathtt{A}+b}^{2}x_{0}^{3}\,,

(B.30)

with variables

x=(x_{0},\ldots,x_{\mathtt{A}})
and
t=(t_{1},\ldots,t_{\mathtt{A}+\mathtt{B}})
.

We estimate the sublevels of
\kappa\mapsto f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa)
using Theorem
B.3
.
The set
X:=[-1,1]^{2\mathtt{A}+\mathtt{B}+1}
is a closed ball of
\mathbb{R}^{2\mathtt{A}+\mathtt{B}+1}
.
The function
f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}:X\times\mathcal{I}\to\mathbb{R}
is continuous and subanalytic.
Then we define the non-zero real analytic function

\rho(x,t):=x_{0}\prod_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}x_{a}t_{a}\,\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq\mathtt{A}}\Big[\big(gx_{a}^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}t_{a}^{2}x_{0}^{6}\big)x_{b}^{6}-\big(gx_{b}^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}t_{b}^{2}x_{0}^{6}\big)x_{a}^{6}\Big]\,.

(B.31)

We observe the following lemma.

Lemma B.7
.

There exist positive constants
c(\mathtt{A}):=c(\mathtt{A},g,\gamma,h)
,

C(\mathtt{A}):=C(\mathtt{A},g,\gamma)>0
such that, for any

x(\vec{n},\vec{m}):=(x_{a}(\vec{n},\vec{m}))_{a=0,\ldots,\mathtt{A}}\,,\quad t(\vec{n},\vec{m}):=(t_{1}(\vec{n}),\dots,t_{\mathtt{A}}(\vec{n}),t_{\mathtt{A}+1}(\vec{m}),\dots,t_{\mathtt{A}+\mathtt{B}}(\vec{m}))\,,

(B.32)

defined by (
B.26
), it results

c(\mathtt{A}){\Big(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}+\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}m_{b}\Big)^{-\tau_{2}}}\leq|{\rho(x(\vec{n},\vec{m}),t(\vec{n},\vec{m}))}|\leq C(\mathtt{A})\Big(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}+\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}m_{b}\Big)^{-1}

(B.33)

with
\tau_{2}:=\mathtt{A}+1+12\binom{\mathtt{A}}{2}
.

Proof.

The upper bound (
B.33
) directly follows by (
B.27
).
The lower bound (
B.33
) follows by (
B.27
) and
the fact that, since
1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}
are all distinct,

\displaystyle\Big|(gx_{a}^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}t_{a}^{2}x_{0}^{6})x_{b}^{6}-(gx_{b}^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}t_{b}^{2}x_{0}^{6}))x_{a}^{6}\Big|

\displaystyle=x_{0}^{12}(\vec{n},\vec{m})(gn_{a}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\tanh(\mathtt{h}n_{a}))(gn_{b}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\tanh(\mathtt{h}n_{b}))\Big|\frac{n_{a}^{3}}{gn_{a}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\tanh(\mathtt{h}n_{a})}-\frac{n_{b}^{3}}{gn_{b}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\tanh(\mathtt{h}n_{b})}\Big|

\displaystyle\geq x_{0}(\vec{n},\vec{m})^{12}\,g^{2}\,\min_{y\geq 1}\Big(\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}y}\frac{y^{3}}{gy+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\tanh(\mathtt{h}y)}\Big)|n_{a}-n_{b}|\geq c\,x_{0}(\vec{n},\vec{m})^{12}

for some constant
c>0
, having used
in the last passage that, for any
y\geq 1
,
g>0
,
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathtt{h}>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}y}\frac{y^{3}}{gy+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}\tanh(\mathtt{h}y)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4y^{2}(8gy+3\gamma^{2}\tanh(\mathtt{h}y)-\gamma^{2}\mathtt{h}y\,{\rm sech}^{2}(\mathtt{h}y))}{(4gy+\gamma^{2}\tanh(\mathtt{h}y))^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2y^{2}}{16g^{2}y^{2}+\gamma^{4}}\big(8g+\gamma^{2}(3\tanh(\mathtt{h}y)-\mathtt{h}y\,{\rm sech}^{2}(\mathtt{h}y))\big)\geq\frac{2}{16g^{2}+\gamma^{4}}8g

using that
the function
3\tanh(y)-y\,{\rm sech}^{2}(y)
is
positive for
y>0
.
∎

We show now that the assumptions (H1) and (H2) of Theorem
B.3
hold true.

Verification of (H1).

By (
B.31
), if
\rho(x,t)\neq 0
then, by (
B.31
),

x_{a}\neq 0\,,\ \forall a=0,\ldots,\mathtt{A}\,,\quad t_{a}\not=0\,,\ \forall a=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}\,.

(B.34)

In particular on the set
\{(x,t)\in X\colon\rho(x,t)\neq 0\}\times Y
the function
f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}
in (
B.30
) is real analytic.

Verification of (H2).

For any
(x,t)
such that

\rho(x,t)\neq 0
,
the analytic function
\kappa\mapsto f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa)

possesses only a finite number of zeros as a
consequence of the next lemma.

Lemma B.8
.

For any
(x,t)

such that
\rho(x,t)\neq 0
,
the analytic
function
\kappa\mapsto f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa)
is not identically zero in
\mathcal{I}
.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that there exists

(x,t)\in X\,,\ x=(x_{a})_{0\leq a\leq\mathtt{A}}\,,\ t=(t_{a})_{0\leq a\leq\mathtt{A}+\mathtt{B}}\quad\text{with}\quad\rho(x,t)\neq 0\quad\text{such \ that}\quad f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa)=0

for any
\kappa
in the interval
\mathcal{I}
.
Then, by analyticity, the function
\kappa\mapsto f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa)
is identically zero also on the larger interval
(-\delta,+\infty)
where

\delta:=\min_{1\leq a\leq\mathtt{A}}t_{a}^{2}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}x_{0}^{6}
.
Note that
\delta>0
as
t_{a}^{2}
,
x_{0}^{6}>0
by (
B.34
).
In particular, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
, all the derivatives

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa)\equiv 0
are zero in the interval

\kappa\in(-\delta,+\infty)
.

We now compute such derivatives at
\kappa=0
differentiating (
B.30
).
The derivatives of
the function
\lambda(y,s,x_{0},\kappa)
defined in (
B.29
)
are given by, for suitable constants
C_{l}\neq 0
,

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}\lambda(y,s,x_{0},\kappa)=C_{l}y^{6l}\,\lambda(y,s,x_{0},\kappa)^{1-2l}\,,\quad\forall l\in\mathbb{N}\,.

Thus we obtain

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}\lambda(y,s,x_{0},\kappa)|_{\kappa=0}=C_{l}\,\mu(y,s,x_{0})^{l}\,\lambda(y,s,x_{0},0)\quad\text{where}\quad\mu(y,s,x_{0}):=\frac{y^{6}}{gy^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}s^{2}x_{0}^{6}}\,,

(B.35)

and, recalling (
B.30
),

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa)|_{\kappa=0}=C_{l}\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}c_{a}t_{a}\mu(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0})^{l}\,\lambda(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0},0)\,,\quad\forall l\in\mathbb{N}\,.

As a consequence, the conditions

\partial_{\kappa}^{l}f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa)|_{\kappa=0}=0
for any

l=1,\ldots,\mathtt{A}
imply that

A(x,t)\vec{r}=0

(B.36)

where
A(x,t)
is the
\mathtt{A}\times\mathtt{A}
matrix

A(x,t):=\begin{pmatrix}\mu(x_{1},t_{1},x_{0})\lambda(x_{1},t_{1},x_{0},0)&\cdots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},t_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})\lambda(x_{\mathtt{A}},t_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0},0)\\
\mu(x_{1},t_{1},x_{0})^{2}\lambda(x_{1},t_{1},x_{0},0)&\cdots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},t_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})^{2}\lambda(x_{\mathtt{A}},t_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0},0)\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mu(x_{1},t_{1},x_{0})^{\mathtt{A}}\lambda(x_{1},t_{1},x_{0},0)&\ldots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},t_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})^{\mathtt{A}}\lambda(x_{\mathtt{A}},t_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0},0)\end{pmatrix}

and
\vec{r}
is the vector

\vec{r}:=\begin{bmatrix}r_{1}\\
\vdots\\
r_{\mathtt{A}}\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad r_{a}:=c_{a}t_{a}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\,,\ \forall\,a=1,\dots,\mathtt{A}\,,

because by assumption each
c_{a}\neq 0
and (
B.34
) holds.
Since
\vec{r}\neq 0
, we deduce by (
B.36
)
that the matrix
A(x,t)
has zero determinant. On the other hand, by the
multilinearity of the determinant,

\displaystyle\det A(x,t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}\mu(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0})\lambda(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0},0)\det\begin{pmatrix}1&\cdots&1\\
\mu(x_{1},t_{1},x_{0})&\cdots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},t_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mu(x_{1},t_{1},x_{0})^{\mathtt{A}-1}&\ldots&\mu(x_{\mathtt{A}},t_{\mathtt{A}},x_{0})^{\mathtt{A}-1}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\prod_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}\mu(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0})\lambda(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0},0)\,\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq\mathtt{A}}(\mu(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0})-\mu(x_{b},t_{b},x_{0}))\,.

(B.37)

The condition
\rho(x,t)\neq 0
implies, by (
B.34
), (
B.35
) and
(
B.29
),
that

\prod_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}\mu(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0})\lambda(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0},0)\neq 0\,,

and, in view of
(
B.37
), the determinant

\det A(x,t)=0
if only if
\mu(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0})=\mu(x_{b},t_{b},x_{0})

for some
1\leq a<b\leq\mathtt{A}
.
By the definition of the function
(y,s,x_{0})\to\mu(y,s,x_{0})
in (
B.35
) it follows that

\mu(x_{a},t_{a},x_{0})=\mu(x_{b},t_{b},x_{0})\quad\Rightarrow\quad\big(gx_{a}^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}t_{a}^{2}x_{0}^{6}\big)x_{b}^{6}-\big(gx_{b}^{2}x_{0}^{4}+\frac{\gamma^{2}}{4}t_{b}^{2}x_{0}^{6}\big)x_{a}^{6}=0\,.

In view of (
B.31
) this contradicts
\rho(x,t)\neq 0
.
∎

We have verified assumptions (H1) and (H2) of Theorem
B.3
.
We thus conclude that
there are
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha_{0},\delta,C>0
, such that for any
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
, any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
N\geq N_{0}
, any
(x,t)\in X
with
\rho(x,t)\neq 0
,

{\rm meas}\big\{\kappa\in\mathcal{I}\colon\ \ \ |f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x,t,\kappa)|\leq\alpha|\rho(x,t)|^{N}\big\}\leq C\alpha^{\delta}\,|\rho(x,t)|^{N\delta}\,.

(B.38)

For
\vec{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{A}}
with
1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}
and

\vec{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{\mathtt{B}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{B}}
we consider the set

\mathcal{B}_{\vec{c},\vec{d},\vec{m},\vec{n}}(\alpha,N):=\left\{\kappa\in\mathcal{I}\colon\ \ \ |f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x(\vec{n},\vec{m}),t(\vec{n},\vec{m}),\kappa)|\leq\alpha|\rho(x(\vec{n},\vec{m}),t(\vec{n},\vec{m}))|^{N}\right\}

(B.39)

where
x(\vec{n},\vec{m})

and
t(\vec{n},\vec{m})

are defined in (
B.32
).
By (
B.33
)
we deduce that
\rho(x(\vec{n},\vec{m}),t(\vec{n},\vec{m}))\neq 0
, and
(
B.38
) implies that

{\rm meas}\,\mathcal{B}_{\vec{c},\vec{d},\vec{m},\vec{n}}(\alpha,N)\leq C\alpha^{\delta}|\rho(x(\vec{n},\vec{m}),t(\vec{n},\vec{m}))|^{N\delta}\,.

(B.40)

Consider the set

\mathcal{K}(\alpha,N):=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{A}},\,1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}\\
\vec{m}=(m_{1},\dots m_{\mathtt{B}})\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{B}}\\
\vec{c}\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{A}},\,|\vec{c}|_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{M}\\
\vec{d}\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{B}},|\vec{d}|_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{M}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{B}_{\vec{c},\vec{d},\vec{m},\vec{n}}(\alpha,N)\subset\mathcal{I}\,.

(B.41)

By (
B.40
) and
(
B.33
) one gets

{\rm meas}\,\mathcal{K}(\alpha,N)\leq C(\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M})\alpha^{\delta}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}}\in\mathbb{N}\\
m_{1},\dots,m_{\mathtt{B}}\in\mathbb{N}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}+\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}m_{b})^{N\delta}}\leq C^{\prime}(\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M})\alpha^{\delta}

(B.42)

for some finite constant
C^{\prime}(\mathtt{A},\mathtt{M})<+\infty
, provided

N\delta>\mathtt{A}+\mathtt{B}
.
We fix

\underline{N}:=[(\mathtt{A}+\mathtt{B})\delta^{-1}]+1\quad\text{and define}\quad{\mathcal{K}}_{\alpha}:=\mathcal{K}(\alpha,\underline{N})

whose measure satisfies

|{\mathcal{K}}_{\alpha}|=O(\alpha^{\delta})
, in view of (
B.42
).

In conclusion,
for any
\kappa\in\mathcal{I}\setminus\mathcal{K}_{\alpha}
, for any

\vec{n}=(n_{1},\ldots,n_{\mathtt{A}})
with

1\leq n_{1}<\ldots<n_{\mathtt{A}}
and
\vec{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathtt{B}}
,
any

\vec{c}\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{A}}
with
|\vec{c}\,|_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{M}

and
\vec{d}\,\in(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})^{\mathtt{B}}
with
|\vec{d}\,|_{\infty}\leq\mathtt{M}
,
it results,
by (
B.41
) and (
B.39
), that

|{f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}\,(x(\vec{n},\vec{m}),t(\vec{n},\vec{m}),\kappa)}|>\alpha|\rho(x(\vec{n},\vec{m}),t(\vec{n},\vec{m}))|^{\underline{N}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{rho.estgh}}}{{\geq}}\frac{c(\mathtt{A})\alpha}{\left(\sum_{a=1}^{\mathtt{A}}n_{a}+\sum_{b=1}^{\mathtt{B}}m_{b}\right)^{\tau_{2}\underline{N}}}\,.

(B.43)

Recalling the definition of

f_{\vec{c},\vec{d}}
in (
B.30
) and
x_{0}(\vec{n},\vec{m})
in (
B.26
),
the lower bound (
B.43
)
implies (
B.24
) with
\tau:=\tau_{2}\underline{N}-3
(cfr. (
B.28
))
and re-denoting
\alpha c(\mathtt{A})\leadsto\alpha
.
∎

Hamiltonian and Dispersive PDEs
.
